# Project State

## Project Reference

See: .planning/PROJECT.md (updated 2026-07-22) · .planning/ROADMAP.md (v2.5 milestone, Phases 112-119) · .planning/REQUIREMENTS.md (38 v1 reqs, 38/38 mapped) · .planning/research/SUMMARY.md (v2.5 research, HIGH confidence)

**Core value:** One pmcp server binary transparently serves both MCP 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28 clients via per-request negotiation — v2 as the strategic primary path (stateless/Lambda-first, Tasks, MCP Apps), v1 as a cleanly severable compatibility layer. The whole milestone stays additive (2.x minor).
**Current focus:** Phase 119 — documentation-three-shapes-v2-migration

(the 33 reads were already bounded by 116-06/07/11/12) and failed only

predicted **7** — it was measured 2026-08-02, before 116-06/07/12 added the `rendered_source_chain`
helpers. Four `ALLOWLIST` entries were added, which is the DESIGNED closure for that list (a change
detector over a justified population), NOT the forbidden move — that is `WHOLE_BODY_ALLOWLIST`, a
prohibition whose empty state is its floor.

**All three controls RUN, with the third labelled as a limit.** (1) Negative: reverting
`read_token_body` to `.bytes().await` fired `no_unbounded_whole_body_read_over_peer_supplied_bytes`
at `:695` naming `src/client/auth.rs:828` — and matched a rustfmt-split chain, on an auth file.
(2) Anti-vacuity in the direction that CAN fail (cognito dropped from `EXTRA_SCOPE`, retained in
`REQUIRED_FILES`): FOUR tests failed at `:170`, "scope discovery lost
src/server/auth/providers/cognito.rs". (3) Counter-control (dropped from `REQUIRED_FILES`,
`EXTRA_SCOPE` intact): PASSED, as expected, and is recorded as the measured LIMIT — that guard
protects discovery, not the requirement list — never as evidence.

**⚠ HARNESS/HOST NOTES FOR THE NEXT EXECUTOR.** `SSL_CERT_FILE=target/116-verify/cacert.pem` did NOT
exist at session start and had to be regenerated (`security find-certificate -a -p

timeout unless launched as `nohup CMD > log 2>&1 < /dev/null & disown` at the TOP LEVEL of a Bash
call — inside a subshell `disown` fails and the process dies. **Never point two runs at one log
path:** a killed run's `Terminated: 15` and a later run's success banner landed in the same file and
that log had to be discarded rather than interpreted. The one `rust-analyzer` seen was serena's
(parent = uv python), 15 s CPU over 2h17m — NOT the Zed fault; do not read a bare non-zero `pgrep`
count as that fault.

209s with it quit) and caused a multi-hour cargo build-lock deadlock.

file's module doc). D-18's detection is correspondingly narrower than the specification's
"detected via updated protected resource metadata". **`116-15` must not book AUTH-03 without
quoting this.**

**⚠ THE REJECTION PATH WAS AN ECHO CHANNEL AND AN UNBOUNDED READ, AND NEITHER WAS IN THE PLAN'S
TASK LIST AS A DEFECT.** `response.text().await.unwrap_or_default()` interpolated the whole
authorization-server-controlled body verbatim into the error message. Both fixed under Rule 2
(`collect_reqwest_body_within_cap` + `MAX_DCR_ERROR_FIELD_CHARS = 200`). The negative control is
the lesson: with the raw body echoed, `a_rejected_registration_names_the_status_the_sent_type_and_the_sent_redirect_uri`
**still PASSED** — every substring it asserts is present in a message that also leaks everything.
**A presence assertion is not a detector for an echo channel; only an absence assertion is.**

**⚠ AN ATOMIC RENAME PREVENTS A TORN FILE, NOT A LOST UPDATE — and the obvious test does not
detect the difference.** Every mutation goes through one private `with_snapshot_mut`
(`tokio::sync::Mutex` in-process + an `O_EXCL` advisory lock file across processes; reads take no
lock). The negative control (**5 breaks at once, `--no-fail-fast`, 29 tests run: 20 passed,
9 failed**) measured which tests are real detectors. Under the read-before-lock break,
`two_concurrent_saves_on_one_store_both_survive` — the `tokio::join!` shape the plan specified —
**still PASSED**, because one task has no await point between its read and its write.
`a_waiter_reads_the_document_the_lock_holder_left_behind` (holder writes WHILE the waiter is
blocked) and `two_instances_over_one_path_...` are the two that failed. A `join!` over two saves is
not a lost-update detector.

**⚠ "EXACTLY ONE WRITE" IS UNOBSERVABLE THROUGH THE FILESYSTEM — the plan's same-bytes criterion
does NOT fail when the override is deleted.** Measured: removing the `save_with_issuer` override
leaves `save_with_issuer_is_one_write_that_makes_both_observable` **passing** (the two-call default
produces byte-identical output). A `tracing` DEBUG event per atomic write
(`CREDENTIAL_WRITE_EVENT_TARGET`) plus a counting `Subscriber` in the test was added; it measures
the two-call baseline as **2** before claiming **1**, and it is the only test the break fails.

**⚠ D-116-LINT — THE PHASE'S OWN CLAUSE-(b) CLIPPY COMMAND REPORTED EXIT 0 ON GATE-RED CODE.**
Measured, not reasoned. Clause (b) (`cargo clippy … -W clippy::pedantic -W clippy::nursery`) passed
clean on a two-arm `match` that `make lint` rejected with `error: clippy::single_match_else` →
`make[1]: *** [lint] Error 101`. Cause: `make lint` sets `RUSTFLAGS="-D warnings"`, which promotes
those same `-W` pedantic lints to hard errors. Adding `-D warnings` to clause (b) alone is NOT the
fix — it then produces **11 FALSE positives** in `pmcp-widget-utils`/`pmcp-code-mode-derive`, every
one a lint `make lint`'s 28-entry `-A` list explicitly allows. The two diverge in OPPOSITE
directions, so neither dominates. **`make lint` / `make quality-gate` is the authoritative clippy
evidence for every remaining source-touching plan; clause (b) is an inner-loop check only.**

**⚠ D-116-DISK — A NEAR-FULL DISK FAKED 12 DOCTEST LINKER REGRESSIONS.** `make quality-gate`'s

`12 × ld: write() failed, errno=28 (No space left on device)` — `df -h /` showed **1.3 GiB free at
91%**, `target/` at **84 GB**. After `rm -rf target/debug/incremental target/semver-checks
target/wasm32-unknown-unknown` (37 GiB free): **428 passed; 0 failed** = 416 + 12, gate **exit 0**.
Run `df -h /` BEFORE attributing any `cc` link failure to code; reclaim `debug/incremental` (33 GB),
do not `cargo clean`.

**The instrument, and why it is not a fourth restatement: RENAME INVARIANCE.** A metamorphic relation DERIVED from a JSON Schema 2020-12 vocabulary fact — the keys of `properties`/`patternProperties`/`$defs`/`definitions`/`dependentSchemas` are AUTHOR-CHOSEN NAMES with no keyword semantics — therefore normalizing an entry cannot depend on the name it is filed under, and two documents differing only in that name must produce equal normalized SUBTREES (subtrees, not whole documents: the `$ref` strings legitimately differ and normalization never resolves refs). It consults **no `DATA_ONLY_KEYWORDS` list at all**. Landed as `property_normalization_does_not_depend_on_a_subschema_map_key_name` and fuzz **invariant 6** `assert_normalization_is_invariant_under_rename`.

What follows is the defect as it stood before 115-12, kept for the record.

**The gap (pre-115-12):** `normalize_schema_dialect` (`src/server/output_validation.rs:146-165`) rewrites only the ROOT `$schema`. Under Draft 2020-12 a `$schema` on an embedded schema resource (a subschema carrying `$id`) is legal and `jsonschema` 0.49.2 honours it, so a legacy dialect declaration there survives the pin and yields the vacuous accept-everything validator the pin exists to prevent. Reproduced independently twice via `output_validation::fuzz_support::validate_bytes`, including `root-draft07 + embedded (v1,v2) = (Violates, Conforms)` — v2 validating **weaker** than v1. Warn-only validation and author-declared schemas bound the blast radius to a lost diagnostic, not a wire defect.

**Why nothing caught it:** all three defensive layers structurally exclude the triggering shape — `normalization_cases()` case (d) is a nested `$schema` *without* `$id` (the one safe nested shape), `arb_schema_document()` injects only a root `$schema`, and the fuzz target's `is_dialect_neutral` excludes `$ref`/`$defs`/`$id`. A green `make quality-gate` and 660 271 fuzz runs agreed the pin was safe.

**Gap-closure scope:** recursive (not root-only) `$schema` normalization; a fixed regression test for the `$id`-bearing nested case; widened property/fuzz generators; corrected rustdoc at `output_validation.rs:142-145` and the contract invariant in `contracts/mcp-protocol-sdk-v1.yaml` ("never honoured and never used as a vocabulary source" is false as written).

Prior-wave context — **114-15 has landed — TASK-05's central claim is PROVEN over a real socket, per method, and NO production defect was found.** `tests/v2_tasks_security.rs` (8 tests, 1123 lines, **zero production bytes changed** — `git diff --stat HEAD~2..HEAD -- src/ crates/ Cargo.toml Cargo.lock` is EMPTY) drives TWO authenticated bearer principals against ONE `StreamableHttpServer` and shows B cannot READ, FEED or CANCEL A's task on any of `tasks/get` / `tasks/update` / `tasks/cancel`. **Indistinguishability is MEASURED, not asserted:** each refusal test fires the SAME method a second time against a never-minted id, in-test on the same server, and compares `error.code` + `error.message` + `error.data` — a hard-coded sentence would prove only that the test and the code agree on a string. **`grep -c ClientBuilder` is 0** — the threat frame is a VALID caller holding a guessed or leaked id, which a `pmcp::Client` cannot construct because it only asks about ids the server handed it. **THE FIXTURE FACT A FUTURE TASKS/UPDATE TEST MUST NOT SIMPLIFY: `task_input_snapshot` answers `NotFound` for a task with no recorded `inputRequests`, so a `working` task refuses EVERY caller — including its owner — and a cross-caller refusal against one proves NOTHING about ownership.** Test 2 therefore runs against a PAUSED task (114-12's `pausing_task_tool`, reached through a real `tools/call`), and reads the store directly afterwards to assert `input_responses` is EMPTY, because `inputResponses` never appears in a `tasks/get` payload. **Every refusal test re-reads the record AS ITS OWNER and asserts `lastUpdatedAt` is unchanged** — that measures "no write landed", and for `tasks/cancel` it is the entire property (v2 answers an EMPTY ack, so an implementation that refused the caller and cancelled anyway passes every assertion about the response bytes). **SEVEN negative controls, all reverted from a `/bin/cp` snapshot with `shasum -a 256` verified; `git checkout --` NOT used (114-14's recorded loss) and `git stash` NOT used.** NC-2 (`tasks/update` scoping) and NC-3 (`tasks/cancel` scoping) each fail EXACTLY their own test; NC-1 (`tasks/get` scoping) fails **three** (tests 1, 4, 7 — tests 4 and 7 read cross-caller through `tasks/get` BY CONSTRUCTION, so this is dependency, not non-independence; the three failing sets stay DISJOINT). **The plan's NC-4 prediction was WRONG in an instructive way:** making wrong-owner distinguishable trips the **CODE** assertion (`Internal` maps to `-32603`), so the message-equality assertion never fired — **NC-5 was added** (`store_error_response`'s v2 `NotFound` arm renders `error.to_string()`, code stays `-32602`, only the message varies) and it fails tests 1-4 on the message equality PLUS test 5 on its id-echo assertion. **NC-6 was added** because NC-3 fails test 3 at the RESPONSE, never at the re-read the test calls load-bearing: NC-6 is the "refused, but cancelled anyway" implementation and fails ONLY that line. **NC-7** replaces the id generator with a counter and fails only test 6. **A control that fails a test on assertion N proves only assertion N** — that is the generalisable lesson. **Test 6 locks the PROPERTY, never the format:** `grep -c 'is_v4\|Version::Random'` is **0**; it measures a per-position entropy LOWER bound over 1024 ids minted through the real `TaskStore::create`, plus non-sequence (mint order != sort order, no numeric adjacency) and non-derivation (adjacent same-owner pairs share <= 8 chars beyond the measured fixed literal). **Both directions measured:** the estimator reports **122.0** on the real generator (exactly at the bar, no slack) and **10.0 = log2(1024)** on a counter — the right NUMBER, not merely the right side of a threshold. Test 7 separates two statements that are easy to confuse — DISJOINTNESS is about the storage key (`make_key`'s owner prefix in `pmcp-tasks`; `validate_access`'s owner comparison in the in-crate store this server actually runs) while `is_anonymous_owner` is about the `allow_anonymous` refusal and treats `""` and `"local"` IDENTICALLY. Test 8 ASSERTS the accepted no-auth-provider shared bucket, with the reason, the scope (fail-closed is about auth-CONFIGURED deployments, row 2) and the `allow_anonymous: false` mitigation in its rustdoc — **it is failed by no control BY CONSTRUCTION and its evidence value is that it stays green.** **A MEASURED PLAN-TEXT DEFECT, the FIFTH in this phase: `cargo nextest run -E 'test(/v2_tasks_security/)'` selects ZERO tests** — nextest's `test()` predicate matches test NAMES, not binary names, so the acceptance criterion "exits 0 with at least 6 tests" is unsatisfiable as written (and with `--no-tests=pass` it exits 0 having run nothing). (6707 lines truncated)` while the run itself was complete; re-running as **`/usr/bin/make`** gave the real 8238-line log. Prior-wave context — **114-14 has landed — `tasks/update` DELIVERS, and SPEC-RECHECK row 19 is CLOSED.** A v2 client can now feed a paused task and watch it resume, proven over a real socket by `tests/v2_tasks_update.rs` (17 tests). The order is the security property: **parse RAW → bound → decode kind-directed → the backend's single atomic write → an EMPTY ack.** **`inputResponses` is read into a `serde_json::Map`, NEVER into `InputResponses`** — that type's `Deserialize` impl IS `try_from_value_untagged`, so deserializing straight into it runs the overlapping guess AT INGRESS, one layer earlier than the route exists to prevent it AND before a single bound has fired. That is D-113-O reintroduced by convenience. The params struct BORROWS the map rather than cloning it, because a clone duplicates up to the 256 KiB total budget before that budget is checked. **The four bounds fire over the raw map before any decode, through ONE new shared `check_input_responses_map_bounds` in `types::mrtr`** — and `extract_input_responses` was refactored to call it too, so **the MRTR ingress is now bounds-before-decode as well**; the single observable change is that an over-bound entry anywhere wins over an earlier undecodable one (the cheaper refusal, and the one an attacker probes). `check_input_response_bounds` moved private → `pub(crate)`; `grep -c 'fn check_input_response_bounds' src/` is **1**, no new constant, and **`MAX_REQUEST_STATE_LEN` is deliberately excluded AND deliberately not re-exported beside its four siblings** — exporting it next to them is how a fifth test gets written asserting a bound this route correctly does not enforce. **Kinds come from `TaskStore::task_input_snapshot` and nowhere else**, typed with `InputResponse::decode_for`; a key the record does not hold is IGNORED (spec prose, and it is CLIENT-chosen so it is never rendered), a key it holds whose value will not decode is REFUSED with a `-32602` naming the key taken via `get_key_value` on the RECORD side. **THE TRAP THE NEXT INTERNAL-ROUTE METHOD WILL HIT, measured off a real socket rather than predicted:** `tasks/update` rides the crate-private internal-request route, which **bypasses `process_client_request`** — the site that writes `resultType` + `_meta.serverInfo`. The first green run of `tasks_update_ack_is_empty` read `Null` where `"complete"` was required, and pmcp's OWN v2 client tolerates an absent `resultType` (Phase 112's absent-means-complete), so it would have shipped silently. `Server::handle_tasks_update` now calls `inject_v2_result_envelope` directly, exactly as `build_discover_response` already did. **Any method added on that route must do the same.** **NINE negative controls, all reverted, `shasum -a 256 -c` OK, `git stash` not used; masking check RUN and all nine failing sets pairwise DISTINCT; every one of the 17 tests is failed by at least one control** — that was NOT true after the plan's three, which is why NC-4…NC-9 exist. **Two of the plan's three predictions were WRONG:** NC-1 (untagged decoder substituted) fails **2 integration + 1 lib**, not "test 5 only" — `never_runs_the_untagged_decoder_on_ingress` is the second by construction; NC-3 (transition on a partial set) fails **2**, not "test 2 only", because the partial first delivery in `ignores_an_already_answered_key` then resumes the task and the replay hits `InvalidTransition`. NC-2 was the one correct prediction. **A SELF-INFLICTED LOSS WORTH CARRYING: `git checkout -- <file>` reverted a negative control to the last COMMIT and destroyed the not-yet-committed property-test module.** Recovered by re-appending; every subsequent control reverted from a `/bin/cp` scratchpad snapshot. `git checkout --` is only a safe revert when the pristine state is the last commit. **FUZZ: a real, recorded, FALSIFIED campaign.** `cargo +nightly fuzz build/run fuzz_tasks_update` — build exit 0/0 warnings; **20 000 runs, `-seed=114014`, exit 0**, `cov: 1960 ft: 5379`, corpus 19 seeded → 616, **artifacts dir EMPTY**. Falsifiability control: deleting the seam's bounds pre-check makes it CRASH with `accepted 65 entries, over the 64 bound — the bounds pre-check did not run` and write an artifact; reverted and re-run green. Invariants 2 and 4 are re-derived INSIDE the target from its own parse, so the campaign is evidence rather than production agreeing with itself. The seam is gated `cfg(any(feature = "fuzzing", test))` — `doc(hidden)` alone is invisible to `cargo public-api` and therefore a VACUOUS fence (113-19) — and `pub(crate) mod task_dispatch` is widened to `pub mod` under `fuzzing` only, verbatim `request_state`'s shape. **`make quality-gate` exit 0 — 4866 passed / 0 failed / 81 ignored across 288 result lines**, 0 non-`ok.` lines, 0 truncation markers, and **0 occurrences of the D-114-A keychain flake** (run with `NEXTEST_TEST_THREADS=4`; disk at 86%). Attributable delta: +3 result lines, +33 passed. `make lint` exit 0 — and it found **three** things bare `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` did not (two `doc_markdown`, one `items_after_statements`, and `unreachable_pub`+`dead_code` on the fuzz seam under `cfg(test)` alone, whose allow is scoped to `not(feature = "fuzzing")` so the REAL fuzz build keeps full analysis). `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev aa651f74^` **223/223 no update required**; `cargo public-api diff` **Removed (none), Changed (none), Added (none)** — the four `pmcp::testing` bound re-exports sit behind the `testing` feature; `make wasm-build` exit 0 with **one** warning naming a touched symbol, `check_input_responses_map_bounds is never used`, which is a NEW member of a pre-existing **38-strong** dead block (essentially all of `types::mrtr` is dead on wasm by D-14 — `extract_mrtr_params`, `extract_input_responses`, `logical_name_of` and all four bound constants warn identically and all predate this plan); `pmat` **0 violations in `src/`**, 5 at `.summary.violations` where the extra over 114-19's 4 is `no_source_site_routes_tasks_update_through_the_mrtr_ingress` at cog 33 — **114-13's, verified not argued**: the function hashes IDENTICALLY out of `git show aa651f74^:` and out of the tree. `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` byte-unchanged; `fuzz/Cargo.toml` gains a `[[bin]]` block only; `crates/pmcp-tasks` **zero-byte diff with 514/514 passing** — so a DynamoDB/Redis-backed deployment gets `tasks/update` delivery with no change, because the decode and the bounds live above the `serde_json::Value` seam. `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **untouched (0-byte diff)**, `requirements mark-complete` deliberately NOT run (TASK-01..06 flip as a GROUP; `## Verdict` still `PENDING`); no `tasks/*` row in `MRTR_METHODS`. New deferrals **D-114-M** (a `TaskRouter` serving `tasks/update` performs its OWN decode unaided — kind-direction is ENFORCED for `TaskStore` and only DOCUMENTED for `TaskRouter`; closing it means widening a legacy-experimental trait) and **D-114-N** (a store that cannot accept inputs on a router-less server reuses the frozen `TASKS_NOT_ENABLED` rather than minting a fifth `-32601` sentence). **NEXT: 114-15**, and **114-17 now has a complete create → pause → update → resume loop to demonstrate** — server half here, client half `Client::tasks_update` (114-19), and a paused task is reachable from a real `tools/call` via the harness's `pausing_task_tool`.

Prior-wave context — **114-19 has landed — the CLIENT half of the v2 tasks surface is REAL, so CONTEXT.md's locked steer ("the server and client halves land together in this phase") is true rather than aspirational.** A v2 pmcp client decodes every shape 114-11/114-12 emit: the FLAT create payload, the flat `tasks/get` with its status-conditional `result`/`error`/`inputRequests`, and BOTH empty acknowledgements. **The v2 branch is chosen by the `resultType` DISCRIMINATOR and never by shape** — the flat create payload and a `CallToolResult` share no discriminating key, and `CallToolResult::content` carries `#[serde(default)]`, so essentially any object decodes as one; the existing v1 comment already warned against duck-typing and on v2 that warning becomes load-bearing (T-114-99). Every arm is NAMED: `"task"`, the explicit `"complete"` and an ABSENT key (one answer — Phase 112's absent-means-complete), and an unrecognised value, which is that answer too but LOGGED, because an unknown discriminator is protocol skew a developer must see. **`call_tool_with_task` and `call_tool_with_task_and_meta` now share ONE decoder** (`grep -c 'from_value::<CreateTaskResult>'` = **1**, was 2) — they were line-for-line identical and era-awareness would have made a third copy. **`tasks_get` keeps returning `Task` on both eras** via the new `TaskV2::to_v1`, which lives beside `from_v1` because that function's own rustdoc calls itself "the ONLY place the two renames happen" — `ttlMs`→`ttl`, `pollIntervalMs`→`pollInterval`, so an existing poll loop is unchanged against a v2 server. **`tasks_get` decodes only the flat BASE task while `tasks_get_detailed` decodes the strict `DetailedTaskV2`, and the asymmetry is deliberate:** the server's `v2_detailed_task_value` DEGRADES to a bare flat `Task` when a backend cannot supply the detail, and a strict `tasks_get` would turn "I could not read the detail" into "I could not read the task at all". Both go through ONE private `tasks_get_raw_v2`, so the poll loop fetches once per tick and reads the base task AND the terminal/pause detail from the SAME bytes. **`tasks_cancel` on v2 = the new `tasks_cancel_ack` (accepts a bare `{}`) plus ONE follow-up `tasks_get` — NEVER a synthesised `Task`:** cancellation is cooperative and eventually consistent (114-11's own reason for adding no server-side wait), so the returned status MAY still be `working`, and a fabricated `cancelled` would be the client lying about a fact only the server holds; the test asserts `working` comes back, i.e. that the invented answer is not what happens. **`tasks_result` and `tasks_list` are NOT deleted** (semver, and v1 still serves both) — each fails fast LOCALLY on v2 through one shared guard minting `Error::retired_on_v2`, the SAME marker the Phase-113 subscriptions retirement uses, so `is_retired_on_v2()` is ONE typed check across both retired families. Replacements are honest, not symmetric: `tasks/result`→`tasks/get`, `tasks/list`→`"client-side task tracking"` (there is no v2 list; pointing at `tasks/get`, which answers about one id the caller already holds, would be a lie in the one string a caller has for choosing its next move). **`wait_for_task`'s terminal step is the only era-aware line in the loop:** v1 still calls `tasks/result`, v2 reads the `result` INLINE from the payload the loop already fetched; a `failed` task surfaces its inlined JSON-RPC error through `Error::from_jsonrpc_error` and a `cancelled` one errors, both because `CallToolResult::content` is `#[serde(default)]` and a permissive decode yields a well-formed EMPTY SUCCESS. **`wait_for_task_with_inputs` reuses that loop WHOLESALE** — both are thin callers of one private `poll_task_to_terminal(.., Option<responder>)`, so the classifier match (no `_` arm), `MIN_POLL_MS`, the budget clamp and the `web_time::Instant` clock are literally the same code, which makes T-114-102 mitigated by construction rather than by a second implementation that happens to agree. Its round bound REUSES the configured `mrtr_round_limit` (default 8) rather than minting a constant, so no `const _: () = assert!` was needed. `tasks_update` sends via `send_untyped_request` (no `ClientRequest` variant, by design) and is v2-only. **`"task"`/`"complete"` moved to `types::mrtr` as `pub(crate)` constants** — `ResponseDisposition::as_wire_str` lives in `server::core`, which is `cfg(not(wasm32))`, and the client compiles ON wasm, so the plan's "if the server constant is not reachable, introduce ONE shared constant" branch was the one that applied. **ELEVEN negative controls, all reverted, `shasum -a 256 -c` OK on all six touched files, `git stash` not used — and the MASKING CHECK FIRED.** NC-9 (drop `assert_capability` from the v2 `tasks/get` fetch) and NC-11 (drop it from `tasks_update`) failed the IDENTICAL single-test set at the IDENTICAL panic LINE, separable only by reading the panic MESSAGE — so the test was SPLIT into `v2_undeclared_tasks_get_…` / `v2_undeclared_tasks_update_…` and both re-measured at 1 disjoint failure each. **Two of the plan's three predictions were WRONG:** NC-1 predicted "tests 4 and 5" and produces test 4 plus the CANCEL test (test 5 goes through `tasks_get_detailed`, which NC-1 does not touch; the cancel test fails because `tasks_cancel`'s v2 arm re-reads through `tasks_get`); NC-2 predicted "test 9 only" and produces **three**. NC-3 was the one correct prediction. All eleven failing sets are pairwise DISTINCT. Four tests are failed by no control BY CONSTRUCTION — they are the v1 controls, whose evidence value is that they stay GREEN. **THE ONE REAL REGRESSION THIS PLAN CAUSED, and the next executor should expect its shape:** `tests/v2_tasks_client.rs :: tasks_list_emits_an_empty_mcp_name` (114-06) used `client.tasks_list(None)` purely as a VEHICLE to observe an empty `Mcp-Name`; making `tasks/list` locally refused removed the vehicle. Repaired at the TRANSPORT (`StreamableHttpTransport::send_raw` with a raw `tasks/list` frame), which is the layer that owns the derivation and is strictly more precise — and a TABLE-level restatement was written, measured as an exact duplicate of the existing `tasks_list_and_tasks_result_carry_no_routing_name`, and DELETED. New deferral **D-114-L**: `tasks/list` and `tasks/result` are the only non-name-bearing `tasks/*` methods and both are now locally refused, so **a pmcp v2 CLIENT can no longer emit a non-name-bearing `tasks/*` method at all**; future header-derivation controls must drop to the transport layer. **Gate:** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exit 0; `make lint` exit 0 (×3); `make build` exit 0; `make test-unit`/`test-doc`/`test-property`/`test-examples` exit 0; `make pmcp-package-gate`/`audit`/`check-todos`/`check-unwraps`/`validate-always`/`purity-check`/`comply` exit 0; `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev 22b36b5f` **223/223, no semver update required**; `cargo public-api diff 22b36b5f..HEAD` **Removed (none), Changed (none)** with exactly 5 ADDITIVE items (`tasks_cancel_ack`, `tasks_get_detailed`, `tasks_update`, `wait_for_task_with_inputs`, `TaskV2::to_v1`) — **`ToolCallResponse` untouched** (T-114-105); no `tasks/*` row in `MRTR_METHODS` and both method tables are byte-identical to plan-start. **`make quality-gate` DID NOT reach exit 0, and it is D-114-A, not a regression — addendum 2 recorded:** every full run loses EXACTLY ONE test to the pre-existing native-roots `.expect` at `src/shared/streamable_http.rs:458` (`no native root CA certificates found` / `Os(code: -36)`), **even unsandboxed with `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1`**, and **the identity of the failing test MOVES between runs** (`session_validation_tests::test_double_initialization_rejected` → `sse_middleware_integration::test_middleware_modifies_request_headers`) while the panic site does not. A regression does not relocate itself. **Run the gate legs individually, grep every failure for that string FIRST, and re-run the single failing binary alone before bisecting.** **NEXT: 114-13** (`tasks/update` routing — you and 114-14 own SPEC-RECHECK row 19's empty ack; the CLIENT half now exists and EXPECTS an empty ack, so a non-empty `UpdateTaskResult` will break `v2_empty_update_ack_is_not_a_decode_error`), then **114-14**. **114-17 must read the `## For 114-17` section of `114-19-SUMMARY.md`** — it states the exact client-side surface, the two-round-trip cost of `tasks_cancel`, the responder signature, and the facts the example must not fight.

Prior-wave context — **114-12 has landed — v2 task creation is REACHABLE, and SPEC-RECHECK row 39 (DQ1) is closed.** A v2 `tools/call` from a client that declared `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks` now mints a real, POLLABLE task handle, proven over a live socket by `tests/v2_tasks_create.rs` (7 tests) — including a `tasks/get` on the returned id, so the handle is demonstrably usable rather than merely well-shaped. **The trigger is ONE era-aware type, `task_dispatch::CreateTrigger{V1TaskField, V2ClientDeclaration}`, replacing the bare `task_requested: bool`** — a variant carrying its own fact, because two adjacent booleans at a call site is precisely how the wrong one gets passed. Its v2 arm REUSES `TaskDispatch::declares_tasks_extension`, the SAME predicate `route_tasks_endpoint`'s case-3 `-32021` refusal uses, off the already-resolved `ProtocolContext` — there is **no second `params._meta` read anywhere on the create path**. **Each era IGNORES the other's trigger, asserted in both directions:** a v2 request carrying the v1 `task` field but no declaration does NOT create; a v1 request carrying a declaration but no `task` field does NOT create. **The complete gate is ONE expression, `TaskDispatch::create_gate`, reached from BOTH dispatchers** — `core.rs`'s divergent inline copy (`req.task.is_some() && self.task_store.is_some() && …` plus its own duplicate task-shape check, under a comment that admitted it was a copy) is DELETED. **The shape chosen, and the reason, because a later plan will be tempted to "unify" it:** the RESPONSE building is NOT shared, the PREDICATE is — `ServerCore::handle_call_tool` returns a `ToolCallOutcome` and builds its envelope one frame up where the request id and the `&mut DispatchEnvelopeClaim` live, while `Server::handle_call_tool` is bound to `Result<Value>`; forcing one response shape would have been the churn, not the fix. `CreateGate` is **three-valued** (`Create`/`NotTaskShaped`/`Closed`) and the middle variant is load-bearing: it lets `ServerCore` keep its tool-authoring `debug!` WITHOUT keeping a second copy of the shape check. **The create → pause loop is CLOSED, which is what makes 114-14 and 114-17 reachable at all:** `store.create()` mints the canonical id AFTER the handler returns, discarding the tool's fabricated `taskId`, so a handler could never associate its input requests with the id the client polls. `build_task_created_response` now re-extracts a THIRD thing — `extract_input_requests` returns the map IFF the value carries BOTH an `input_required` status (decoded through `TaskStatus`, never a re-spelled literal) AND a parseable `inputRequests` object — and records it against `store_id`, the SAME variable the envelope reflects. The terminal-result and pause branches are **mutually exclusive in the source** (`if let … else if let …`), and the `SIGNATURE NOTE` rustdoc names all three re-extractions. **`ResponseDisposition::Task`'s `allow(dead_code)` was ALREADY GONE — verified, not re-implemented:** the enum block was extracted from `git show 6bb89170:` and from the tree and diffed **IDENTICAL**; 114-11 removed it one plan early (D-114-H). **SEVEN negative controls, not the plan's two, and BOTH of the plan's predictions were WRONG.** NC-1 (v2 arm also requires the `task` field) fails **1 and 1b**, NOT "1 and 3" — test 3's DECLARING leg deliberately carries the v1 field, so it still creates. NC-2 (v2 gate unconditional) fails **4 of 7** (1, 1b, 2, 3), not "test 2 only"; the three that stay green are exactly the rows closed by a DIFFERENT clause. NC-3/4/5/6 (v1 arm always fires / drop the `task_support` clause / drop the backend clause / neuter `extract_input_requests`) each fail **exactly one** test — 4, 5, 6, 1b respectively. **THE MASKING CHECK WAS RUN AND IT FIRED:** NC-1 and NC-7 fail the SAME test set, so they were split by PANIC SITE — NC-1 panics in `minted_id` (the CREATE produced no handle), NC-7 (mint under a shadow owner) panics in `result_of(&polled)` (the create SUCCEEDED and the POLL failed). Without NC-7, test 1's `tasks/get` would have been a property no control ever failed. All seven reverted, `shasum -a 256 -c` OK on all three touched files, **`git stash` not used**. **A MEASURED plan-text defect worth carrying: the acceptance criterion `!raw.contains("taskId")` is UNSATISFIABLE, and the reason is structural** — the gate only fires on a TASK-SHAPED value, so "the same tool, same server" FORCES the non-creating leg to text-wrap a payload spelling `taskId`; the real wire was captured to prove it. Replaced by `!raw.contains("\"taskId\":")` — the nine-byte PROTOCOL spelling, which the escaped wrapped text does not contain and a leaked JSON key always does: satisfiable AND strictly more precise. grep the stderr for that string before bisecting anything.** New deferral **D-114-K**: the v2 trigger is per-REQUEST and per-CLIENT, never per-TOOL — a declaring client gets a handle from EVERY task-capable tool and has no per-call opt-out (there is no v2 equivalent of the `task` field). Prior-wave context — **114-11 has landed — the v2 task result surface is REAL, and SPEC-RECHECK row 23 is now closed END TO END on the pmcp side.** 114-10 supplied the egress PERMISSION and dispatched nothing; this plan dispatches. A v2 `tasks/get` on an `input_required` task reads the recorded set through 114-04's `TaskStore::task_input_snapshot` (never the private `TaskRecord`), inlines it as a **TOP-LEVEL** `inputRequests`, and returns `DispatchEnvelopeClaim::TASKS_INPUT_REQUIRED` alongside the response — asserted on **RAW RESPONSE BYTES over a real socket**, with NC-1 (narrowing the `TasksDispatch` grant back to nothing) failing **exactly that one test** in the new suite plus two of 114-10's. **Three shapes landed, era-split, with ONE store/router/no-backend path per route and the era deciding only the SHAPE at the end:** `tasks/get` -> flat `DetailedTask`; `tasks/cancel` -> **empty ack** (cooperative and eventually consistent — deliberately NO wait and NO poll were added to make it look synchronous); `tools/call` create -> flat `Result & Task` with `resultType: "task"`. **`crates/pmcp-tasks` needed NO change to serve v2** (D-11) — verified, not asserted: 514/514 with a zero-byte diff to that crate, so DynamoDB/Redis-backed tasks get the v2 shapes from day one. **The projection types are additive and the per-variant requirement is STRUCTURAL, not a comment:** `TaskDetailV2` is an enum with one variant per `TaskStatus` carrying exactly the key its `$defs` variant marks required, and `DetailedTaskV2` has PRIVATE fields with a constructor that makes the DETAIL authoritative for `status` — a `completed` projection cannot be built without `result` because `TaskDetailV2::Completed {}` does not compile, which is why there is no "completed with no result" test to write. **`ttlMs` is required AND nullable** (`Option<u64>` WITHOUT `skip_serializing_if`, so `None` emits `"ttlMs":null`) while `pollIntervalMs` is genuinely optional (WITH it) — TASK-04's own text enumerates only FOUR required fields and omits `ttlMs`; there are five. **`DispatchEnvelopeClaim` is the second envelope claimant and the plumbing is the part later plans need:** `route_tasks_endpoint`, `maybe_build_task_created` and `build_task_created_response` return `(JSONRPCResponse, DispatchEnvelopeClaim)`; `handle_request_internal` (core) and `handle_client_request` / `process_client_request` / `handle_call_tool` (mod) carry a `&mut DispatchEnvelopeClaim` out-param that only the two claiming arms write; both dispatch sites fold it with the MRTR egress's pair through ONE named rule, `or_egress`. **This is a Rule-3 deviation — `core.rs` and `mod.rs` are two files beyond the declared `files_modified`** — and it was unavoidable: `own_reserved_result_fields` OWNS `resultType` and overwrites whatever a producer writes, so a claim written into the result object is silently discarded, and `handle_request_internal` returned only the response. **SO: 114-12's "supply `ReservedFieldOwner::TasksDispatch` from the tasks route" item is DONE — what remains yours is the v2 create TRIGGER, not the shape, and do NOT add a second path.** **D-114-H is CLOSED one plan early:** both `ResponseDisposition::Task` and `ReservedFieldOwner::TasksDispatch` now have production constructors on every native build (`server::task_dispatch` is gated only on `not(wasm32)` and on no feature), so both `allow(dead_code)` guards were DELETED after measuring `--features full` (exit 0), `--no-default-features --features streamable-http` (no error naming either) and `--no-default-features` (55 pre-existing D-114-E errors, none naming either). **Task-not-found is `-32602` on v2 with ONE oracle-free message** that is identical for absent / wrong-owner / **expired** and never renders the id back. **`Expired` was folded in deliberately — a CORRECTION to the plan text, which named only `NotFound`:** row 29's anti-oracle constraint enumerates expired, and `From<TaskStoreError> for Error` already maps it to `not_found`; leaving it on `-32603` with its own id-bearing message would have made the SHARPER code the thing that leaked. v1 stays `-32603` for every variant. `V1_TASK_PENDING` is untouched — **the plan's acceptance grep `grep -c 'V1_TASK_PENDING'` moved 6 -> 7 and CANNOT pass as written**, because the plan's own action text asks for the rustdoc that adds the seventh occurrence; the intent was measured directly instead and the non-comment EMISSION count is unchanged at **4**. **ELEVEN negative controls, not the plan's two, and the extra nine are the point:** the plan's pair would have left the status set-equality lock, the v1 nested freeze, the v1 error-code freeze, BOTH halves of the `-32602` contract and BOTH `resultType` boundary tests with no evidence any of them is load-bearing. **Two masking findings:** NC-5 and NC-6 initially failed the SAME four tests because NC-5 mutated the code and the message at once — NC-7 was added to isolate the CODE and NC-6 re-scoped to isolate the MESSAGE; and **NC-8 does NOT fail test 11** because test 11's `tasks/get` is on an `input_required` task, which takes the other arm — NC-10 mutates that arm, so the two together cover both arms of one branch. **The plan's own NC-2 prediction was WRONG**: it predicted 5 failures, it produces **10 of 13** (several are fixture dependencies, not independent properties — which is exactly why the isolating controls exist). All 11 reverted, `shasum -a 256 -c` OK on all four touched files, **`git stash` not used**. **`tests/common/v2.rs` was EXTENDED, never forked:** `spawn_tasks_server` is now a wrapper over a new `spawn_tasks_server_with_store` primitive plus a synchronously-completing `isError` tool — `input_required` and `failed` have NO client-facing trigger in this phase, so a shape suite cannot construct two of the five shapes without the store, and `terminal_status_discipline` must exercise the REAL create path rather than poking the store into the answer it asserts. **Gate:** `make quality-gate` **exit 0 — 4759 passed / 0 failed / 80 ignored across 276 result lines**, 0 truncation markers, 0 non-`ok.` lines, and it reconciles to a single test against 114-10's 273/4700: the new `v2_tasks_shapes` binary is `Running` in **three** gate legs (+3 lines, +13 passed) and the 23 new lib tests are counted twice (+46) = **+59**. `make lint` exit 0 (x3); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exit 0; `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev 555f118c` **223/223 no update required**; `cargo public-api diff 555f118c..HEAD` **Removed (none), Changed (none)** — purely additive; `make wasm-build` exit 0 with **0** warnings naming any touched symbol; `pmat` at the inherited **4** violations, **0 in `src/`** (queried at `.summary.violations`; the top-level path still reports 0 vacuously). `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` byte-unchanged; `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **untouched (0-byte diff)** and `requirements mark-complete` deliberately NOT run (TASK-01..06 flip as a GROUP; `## Verdict` still `PENDING`). **`114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` rows 4-10, 11-15, 16/17/18/20, 21-24 and 29 were all updated with what landed and what did NOT** — row 19 (`UpdateTaskResult`'s empty ack) is explicitly still unowned because `tasks/update` is not routed yet; a forward tripwire is armed in `tasks_get_never_carries_result_type_task`. Prior-wave context — **114-10 has landed — SPEC-RECHECK row 23, the highest-severity finding in this phase, is CLOSED on the pmcp side.** The reserved-field registry no longer DERIVES ownership from the disposition; `own_reserved_result_fields` takes an explicit `ReservedFieldOwner{None, Mrtr, TasksDispatch}` threaded from the egress that minted the fields, and **the grant is per-KEY per-OWNER** through one function, `ReservedFieldOwner::may_emit`: `Mrtr` gets `requestState` + `inputRequests`, `TasksDispatch` gets `inputRequests` and NOTHING else (the tasks surface has no continuation token — the persisted record replaces the sealed continuation, D-17), `None` gets neither. The strip loop now visits EVERY reserved key on EVERY path and asks `may_emit`, so a future owner added to the enum cannot gain a key by omission. **The defect was reproduced at RUNTIME before being fixed**, and the reproduction is now the regression test: pre-fix, `cargo nextest … binary_id(pmcp::v2_reserved_fields_tasks)` exited **100** with the required key absent from the emitted bytes while `"resultType":"complete"` and a well-formed `_meta.serverInfo` were still written — i.e. a response that passes any "did it succeed" assertion and that a conformant client rejects — and the `tracing::warn!` fired at target `mcp.v2`, `field = "inputRequests"`, message `removed a handler-supplied reserved result field from a result this egress did not mint`. **The warning even called the server's own field *handler-supplied***: the registry had no vocabulary for a second server-side minter, so it misattributed it. **The ownership claim is made where the write happens** — `seal_input_required` returns `Ok((InputRequired, ReservedFieldOwner::Mrtr))` at the two `insert()` calls it describes; `mrtr_egress`/`fail_mrtr_egress`/`MrtrRound::finish` return the pair; the five production call sites each NAME their owner and the parameter has **no default**. **The vendored artifact was re-checked on disk, not quoted from research:** `$defs.GetTaskResult` is a **flat `allOf`** (`Result & (WorkingTask|InputRequiredTask|…)`), not a `{"task": …}` wrapper, so `inputRequests` really is a TOP-LEVEL result key on v2 — while v1's `GetTaskResult` DOES wrap under `task`; the two eras genuinely differ and that difference is **114-11's**. **SIX negative controls, not the plan's three, and the extra three are the point:** the plan's NC-1/2/3 leave `mrtr_minted_input_requests_still_survives`, `a_v1_result_is_untouched_by_the_registry` and `a_non_object_v2_result_is_untouched` unfailed by ANY control — three of six properties would have been recorded as "locked" with no evidence they are load-bearing. NC-4 (narrow the `Mrtr` grant), NC-5 (delete the era gate), NC-6 (delete the object guard) each fail exactly their own test. Also measured: **NC-1 fails TWO tests, not one** — `tasks_minted_request_state_is_still_stripped` carries BOTH keys in one fixture and asserts one survives while the other is removed, which makes it a second independent detector of the row-23 regression (a strengthening; the plan's prediction predated the test). All six reverted, `shasum -a 256 -c` OK on both files, `git diff --stat -- src/` empty, **`git stash` not used**. **A `pub(crate)` seam was required (Rule 3): `pmcp::testing::{v2_result_envelope, v1_result_envelope}` return an `EnvelopeOutcome{bytes, warnings}`** — the bytes AND the `tracing` warnings together, because the removal is silent by design and absence alone cannot distinguish "stripped" from "never there". Capture is a ~60-line hand-written `tracing::Subscriber` scoped by `with_default` (thread-local), deliberately NOT `tracing-subscriber`, so `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` stay byte-unchanged (T-114-SC). **THE PLAN-TEXT DEFECT WORTH CARRYING: both of its `nextest` selectors are wrong — the FIFTH time in this phase.** `test(/v2_reserved_fields_tasks/)` selects **0** and exits **4**; worse, `test(/v2_mrtr/)` **exits 0 VACUOUSLY** — it selects 5 lib tests whose names happen to contain the token, not the 46-test `pmcp::v2_mrtr` integration binary the plan means. **A green `test(/…/)` run is not evidence the intended suite ran**; use `binary_id(pkg::binary)`. Measured with `-D warnings … --lib`: `--features full` exit **0** without any allow (the seam constructs all three variants); `--no-default-features --features streamable-http` exit **101** with exactly one error, `variant TasksDispatch is never constructed`; `--no-default-features` reports **both** `Mrtr` and `TasksDispatch`. **A PROCESS TRAP THAT PRODUCED A FALSE MEASUREMENT:** an `&&`-chained probe aborted at a non-existent **`/usr/bin/cp`** (macOS has `/bin/cp`; `/usr/bin/ls` and `/usr/bin/cat` are also absent), so the heredoc meant to REMOVE the allows never ran and the subsequent "exit 0" was measured against the unmodified tree — while a `grep -c` in the same invocation printed a plausible-looking count that masked it. **Verify the mutation landed before trusting the measurement it enables.** **Gate:** `make quality-gate` **exit 0 — 4700 passed / 0 failed / 80 ignored across 273 result lines**, 0 truncation markers, 0 non-`ok.` lines, and it reconciles to a single line against 114-09's 270/4694: the new binary is `Running` in **three** gate legs (log lines 2773/4066/5648), two with a filter reporting `running 0 tests` and one running all six → **+3 lines, +6 passed**. `make lint` exit 0 (×4); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exit 0; `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev 164f5f15` **223/223 no update required**; `cargo public-api diff 164f5f15..HEAD` **Removed (none), Changed (none)** with 7 additive `pmcp::testing` items — so the plan's parenthetical "all touched items are private/`pub(crate)`" is right in verdict, wrong in reason; `make wasm-build` exit 0 with **0** warnings naming any touched symbol; `pmat` at the inherited **4** violations, **0 in `src/`** (queried at `.summary.violations` — the top-level path still returns "0 violations" vacuously). All **84** deletion lines enumerated with `/usr/bin/git` and every one an intended replacement. `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **untouched (0-byte diff)** — TASK-04 is implemented at the egress layer but stays unflipped and `requirements mark-complete` was deliberately NOT run (TASK-01..06 flip as a GROUP; `## Verdict` still `PENDING`); **`114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` row 23 was updated** to say what is resolved (pmcp's behaviour) and what is NOT (the draft wire value is still held, and **114-11 still owns emitting the flat `InputRequiredTask` shape** — 114-10 supplied only the egress PERMISSION and dispatches nothing). Prior-wave context — **114-09 has landed: v2 task owner binding now FAILS CLOSED, and the two refusals fire in the required order.** `TaskDispatch::resolve_owner` is era-aware and its v2 arm **IS** `resolve_mrtr_principal` — the same function Phase 113 minted for MRTR, called, not copied — so there is ONE identity table for every v2 ingress path on the server (`Some(sub)`→`sub`; `None`+auth-provider→**REFUSE**; `None`+no-auth-provider→`ANONYMOUS_PRINCIPAL`). `ANONYMOUS_PRINCIPAL`/`MrtrPrincipal` lost their `streamable-http` cfg (now `not(wasm32)` only) so `task_dispatch` could REUSE the value rather than spell a second `""` literal. **`Option<String>` became `OwnerBinding{Owner,Refused}`**: `None` already meant "no task backend", and reusing it for "refused" would have made the fail-closed row indistinguishable from a configuration fact at every call site — the exact 114-08 duplicate-answer failure mode. The v2 arm never calls `TaskRouter::resolve_owner` (its chain reaches a **session id**, which TASK-05 forbids, and a **`client_id`**, which is per-APPLICATION `azp` and would collapse per-user isolation to per-app); both disqualified sources are named in the rustdoc so a later "unify the paths" cleanup has to argue with a written reason. **v1 is byte-frozen** — router delegation and the `"local"` fallback unchanged, only D-10's migration warn added; all 14 `v1_tasks_golden` fixtures green. **The five-case ordered chain** in `route_tasks_endpoint`: (1) retired-on-era `-32601` → (2) no-backend `-32601` → (3) client-did-not-declare `-32021` → (4) unauthenticated-on-auth-configured `-32003` at HTTP 200 → (5) params LAST. Cases 3+4 sit INSIDE the has-backend guard, so a backendless server keeps its own truthful answer. The owner is bound EXACTLY ONCE and passed down as `&str`. No numeric error-code literal in `task_dispatch.rs`; `requiredCapabilities` is built by SERIALIZING the real capability types, so it is an OBJECT and cannot drift from `TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY`. **THE FINDING FOR LATER PLANS: v1's `"local"` and v2's `ANONYMOUS_PRINCIPAL` (`""`) are DISJOINT KEYS.** `is_anonymous_owner` treats them identically for `allow_anonymous`, but `make_key` prefixes by owner — so a v1-created anonymous task is UNREACHABLE from v2. Intended (114-15 asserts both facts separately), and it broke 114-08's test 7, which created on v1 and read on v2 anonymously; that fixture now authenticates BOTH legs and every assertion was kept. **A SECOND MEASURED DEFECT IN THE PLAN TEXT (D-114-F):** the specified ordering probe "send malformed params, assert `-32003` not `-32602`" is UNOBSERVABLE — `ClientRequest` is deserialized at INGRESS, so bad params make the variant not match and the request is answered `-32601 "Method not found"` without ever reaching the refusal chain. Test 4 proves the ordering a STRONGER way instead: identical well-formed params differing only in the credential, where the authenticated leg reaches the store and hears "task not found" while the unauthenticated leg hears `-32003` and learns nothing — i.e. no store or router was consulted, which is what T-114-37 actually requires. **Four negative controls, all reverted:** row 2→anonymous fails `v2_unauthenticated_with_auth_provider_is_refused`; case 4 hoisted above 1–3 fails BOTH the retirement and the `-32021` probes; case 5 hoisted above case 4 fails with `-32603 "task not found"`. **`git stash` not used.** **Gate:** `make quality-gate` **exit 0 — 4694 passed / 0 failed / 80 ignored across 270 result lines** (114-08's 4670 + 16 new tests, lib suite counted twice); `make wasm-build` exit 0 with **0** warnings naming any touched symbol (`task_dispatch` is `cfg(not(wasm32))` — **the wasm path does not reach tasks at all**); `pmat` at the inherited **4** violations, **0 in `src/`**; `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev 27364eb1` **no update required** (`resolve_task_owner` is a private `fn`, everything else `pub(crate)`). `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **byte-unchanged** and `requirements mark-complete` deliberately NOT run (D-18 verdict still `PENDING`; TASK-01..06 flip as a GROUP). `streamable_http_server.rs` untouched, so `-32003` stays off `v2_status_for_code`'s 400 arm. A new SPEC-RECHECK row records the **TASK-05 / D-07-row-3 wording gap**: fail-closed applies to AUTH-CONFIGURED deployments; a no-auth-provider server shares ONE v2 bucket by design, with the deferred configurable proxy-header identity source named as the future closure — **114-18 must carry that qualification when it books TASK-05**. **NEXT: 114-12**, then **114-13**, and **114-10** (row 23) before anything depends on a v2 `tasks/get`; **114-15** proves this plan's work over a real socket in wave 10 — see the `## For 114-15` section of `114-09-SUMMARY.md`, which states the identity table, the fail-closed rule, per-method reachability and the required harness posture so it need not guess.

Prior-wave context — **114-07 has landed AND been closed out by a fresh verification agent: `tasks/update` input delivery is implemented ONCE over `StorageBackend::put_if_version`, so the memory, DynamoDB and Redis backends gain it from the same code — and the policy has since been lifted OUT of that implementation into a `pmcp` function the in-crate store calls too.** **The single most important fact for anyone reading `generic.rs`: it NO LONGER holds its own copy of the accept/ignore/complete partition.** 114-07 wrote it inline; the `/simplify` cleanup pass (`9081be3b`) **extracted** it to `pmcp::server::task_store::partition_input_delivery` (`src/server/task_store.rs:313`), which has **exactly two callers** — pmcp's typed `InMemoryTaskStore::deliver_task_inputs` (`task_store.rs:1279`) and pmcp-tasks' `Value`-shaped `GenericTaskStore::deliver_inputs` (`generic.rs:683`) — verified by grep, not assumed. It was possible with **no manifest change** (`pmcp::server` is ungated, `task_store` is gated only on non-wasm, and pmcp-tasks already depends on pmcp with `default-features = false`). The function reads **key sets only, never values** — that is exactly what lets a typed store and a `serde_json::Map` store share it — and it **mutates and persists nothing**. The deliberate per-store differences were PRESERVED rather than unified: pmcp keeps `can_transition_to` + explicit `InvalidTransition` and an inline `to_rfc3339()`; pmcp-tasks keeps `validate_transition` and `Self::touch`; persistence stays a `DashMap` guard on one side and a `put_if_version` CAS on the other. The same commit **finished the helper lift** 114-07 started: `check_owner`/`check_not_expired`/`touch` were wired into only the 3 newest methods, leaving the identical blocks hand-inlined in `get`, `update_status`, `set_variables`, `set_result` and `complete_with_result`; at HEAD there are **8 / 7 / 7** call sites and **0** remaining inline `record.owner_id != owner_id` blocks, so the security-critical "return `NotFound`, never reveal another owner's task" rule has ONE implementation instead of six copies whose `warn!` text had already begun to differ. And `80f87c85` made the cross-crate JSON mirror **enforced**: `TaskInputDelivery`/`TaskInputSnapshot` gained `Serialize, Deserialize` + `rename_all = "camelCase"`, and a test asserts the derived key sets equal the key sets the generic store's **unchanged** hand-written `json!` literals emit — which is the proof the derives are byte-identical to what was already on the wire (before this, `TaskInputSnapshot`'s snake_case field names would not even have produced `inputRequests`/`inputResponses`). **All three D-13 sites are live and each is proven at RUNTIME, not just by grep** — the blanket `TaskStore` impl for `GenericTaskStore`, the `InMemoryTaskStore` wrapper (F12's forgettable site), and `TaskRouterImpl::handle_tasks_update` — plus a build-time tripwire that `include_str!`s the trait, the blanket impl and the wrapper and fails when a trait method has no forwarding line. **The two negative controls the plan required were never run by the original executor; they were run during close-out and both fired attributably.** NC-1 (delete the `deliver_inputs` delegation from `memory.rs`) **compiles** — that is the whole point — and fails **exactly 3 of 29**: the tripwire *naming `deliver_inputs`*, plus the two runtime twins reporting `Internal("store error: store does not support task input delivery")`, i.e. the precise failure mode the plan predicted (memory-backed deployments broken while the identical DynamoDB/Redis paths work). NC-2 (replace `put_if_version` with an unconditional `put`) fails **exactly 2 of 29**, and **the PAIR is what makes the CAS claim load-bearing**: the always-conflict double proves conflict PROPAGATION but can never prove first-writer-*wins* because it never lets a writer win, so a **barrier-backed** double (both writers read the same version, a `tokio::sync::Barrier` then releases both writes) supplies the other half — under the plain `put` both writers "succeed" and one silently overwrites the other. Both reverted with `shasum -a 256 -c` OK; **`git stash` not used**. **The owner comes from the ARGUMENT and it is proven, not asserted:** `resolve_owner_id` count inside `handle_tasks_update`'s body is **0** (the identifier appears only in the rustdoc explaining why), `TaskUpdateParams` declares **only** `taskId`/`inputResponses`, and `the_router_ignores_an_owner_supplied_in_params` plants BOTH `"ownerId"` and `"owner"` in the payload and asserts refusal. A pre-114 record is read from a **RAW BYTE fixture** and proven still **OPERABLE** (a delivery is accepted as a call, ignores every key, and leaves the task paused), not merely deserializable. Measured, not estimated: a worst-case record — 64 entries, **260,481 B** of `inputResponses` (99.4% of the 262,144 B ingress bound, so the fixture is asserted to be a worst case on BOTH sides) plus metadata, variables, the full request set, a result and an error — serializes to **269,948 B** against DynamoDB's **409,600 B** limit, **139,652 B (34%) headroom**; and each delivery is exactly **ONE** write, so a many-round elicitation cannot become quadratic I/O. **TWO plan-text defects were found and corrected rather than worked around.** (a) The plan's own verify command `cargo nextest run -p pmcp-tasks -E 'test(/input_delivery/)'` selects **ZERO** tests and exits **4** (`Starting 0 tests across 10 binaries (514 tests skipped)`) — nextest's `test()` predicate matches test NAMES, and no test name carries that token; the correct selector `-E 'binary_id(pmcp-tasks::input_delivery)'` gives **exit 0, 29/29**. **This is the THIRD plan in this phase to hit that trap (114-01, 114-02, now 114-07) — stop writing `-E 'test(/…/)'` into plans unless the test NAMES really carry the token.** (b) "all 197 pre-existing tests" is stale by 288: the measured pre-existing count is **485** (514 total − 29 new), and **zero** `#[test]`/`#[tokio::test]` attributes were added or removed anywhere under `crates/pmcp-tasks/src`, so the 485 are provably unmodified and all 29 new tests live in the one new file. **ONE acceptance criterion is genuinely UNMET and it was PROVEN pre-existing rather than argued: `make test-feature-flags` exits 2.** It fails at row 1/4's second sub-command, `cargo clippy -p pmcp-tasks --no-default-features -- -D warnings` → **exit 101**, where 56 `dead_code` warnings in the **ROOT `pmcp` lib** are promoted to errors because that feature selection leaves them callerless. A detached worktree at 114-07's base commit **`4327b246`** with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` produced the **identical** exit 2 / exit 101 / 56 errors / same per-file distribution (`mrtr.rs` 42, `subscriptions.rs` 7, `core.rs` 4, `sse_parser.rs` 2, `mod.rs` 1) — **0 in `crates/pmcp-tasks/`, 0 in `src/server/task_store.rs`**. The named items belong to Phase 113 and 114-05. Logged as **D-114-E** (also a blocker) and NOT fixed: the fix lands in five root files owned by other plans, and it is **invisible to `make quality-gate` and to CI**, which lint the root crate with a generous allow-list — which is why it went unnoticed since Phase 113. a staleness double must keep **stale value / stale absence / converged** distinct. **The store contract 114-14 must dispatch against is stated explicitly in the SUMMARY**: an unknown key is **IGNORED and reported** (the store never inspects kinds or values — kind decoding is 114-14's job above the seam via `TaskInputSnapshot::kind_of`, which returns `None` for a key the server never issued); a terminal **or still-`working`** task yields `InvalidTransition` → **`-32602`**; `NotFound`/`Expired` also → `-32602`; `ConcurrentModification` → **`-32603`**; and **bounds are NOT enforced in the store at all** — four (not five: `MAX_REQUEST_STATE_LEN` bounds the MRTR token and `tasks/update` carries none), all at request ingress before any decode. **NEXT: 114-14** (the `tasks/update` dispatch half — it owns T-114-29's four bounds and the kind-directed decode), then **114-09**, **114-12**, **114-13**, and **114-10** (row 23) before anything depends on a v2 `tasks/get`.
Prior-wave context — **114-06 has landed: the CLIENT half of tasks negotiation is real — a v2 client declares the extension on every request, refuses an un-negotiated `tasks/*` call locally with ZERO bytes on the wire, and sets the spec's `Mcp-Name: <taskId>` routing header through a SECOND name-key table that keeps `tasks/update` out of the pipeline that would delete its payload.** **The Task-1 measurement came out the boring way, and that is the useful finding:** the v2 `_meta` emission already SERIALIZES the struct (`serde_json::to_value(self.v2_client_capabilities())`), it does not hand-build a `json!` object — so 114-03's `extensions` field was already on the wire path and nothing needed rewriting; the work was providing the way to SET it. `ClientBuilder::with_tasks_extension()` inserts `TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY → {}` into a new `declared_extensions` map that `v2_client_capabilities` **MERGES** (not assigns — `ClientCapabilities::default()` carries `extensions: None` *today*, and an assignment would silently discard a future default that pre-seeded the map). The serialize-the-struct property is now PINNED by a test that round-trips the emitted value back through `serde_json::from_value::<ClientCapabilities>`, so a future hand-built object — which would silently drop any newly added field — fails. **The declaration is deliberately v1-INERT** (D-02): v1 advertises the `ClientCapabilities` the caller passed to `initialize`, so injecting there would move every existing caller's handshake bytes — the same "more complete"-looking fix 114-03 already had to refuse for `ClientCapabilities::full()`. **Absence is asserted as key ABSENCE and presence as equality with `{}`**, in both the unit and the integration test, for the reasons 114-03 and 114-05 measured: `skip_serializing_if` makes `"extensions": null` the exact falsy shape a value check would accept, and a presence-only check passes on precisely the change that would break it. **`assert_capability`'s `tasks` arm is now era-SPLIT** — v2 reads `extensions[TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY]` (PRESENCE via `contains_key`, **not** `{}`-equality: an operator-authored richer value still means supported, and refusing it would be the mirror image of the over-removal 114-05's v1 projection deliberately avoids), v1 still reads `capabilities.tasks`. The load-bearing test is the NON-VACUITY guard `v2_tasks_capability_ignores_the_v1_tasks_field`: a v2 client whose stored projection carries `capabilities.tasks = Some(..)` and no extensions entry must **FAIL** — that is the fixture that passed under the old arm, and reading `capabilities.tasks` on v2 would refuse every conformant v2 server because `project_capabilities_for_v2` strips it. Its v1 twin asserts the separation in BOTH directions, including that the v2 spelling does not satisfy v1. The escape hatch was NOT narrowed, the `_ =>` arm's `debug_assert!` count is unchanged at **1**, and **no new error variant** was added — `Error::capability` is reused with the key named in the message (T-114-23). **"Fails fast" is MEASURED twice**: the unit test asserts 0 typed sends and 0 raw bodies; the integration test zeroes its counter AFTER a real `server_discover` round trip so the `0` is attributable to the refused call alone, and its non-vacuity twin drives the SAME call against a projection that DOES carry the extension and asserts **1** send. **The trap this plan existed to avoid was demonstrated, not just described.** `TASK_NAME_BEARING_METHODS` (3 rows, all `TASK_ID_KEY` spelled once) plus `name_bearing_key` (`logical_name_key` first, then the tasks table); `frame_routing_pair` — the ONE shared reader both ends use — resolves through it, so header and body still come from the same bytes (T-113-08). `mrtr_eligible` still reads `MRTR_METHODS` and ONLY `MRTR_METHODS`. **Negative control, run and reverted:** a `tasks/update` row in `MRTR_METHODS` (arity 3→4) fails **exactly three** tests — the Phase-113 lock `mrtr_eligible_is_exactly_three_methods`, `tasks_methods_are_name_bearing_but_not_mrtr_eligible`, and `the_two_name_key_tables_are_disjoint` — while `logical_name_key_table` and `tasks_list_and_result_are_not_name_bearing` stay **GREEN**, which is what makes the control attributable rather than indiscriminate. Reverted from a byte-for-byte copy (`shasum -a 256 -c` OK); **`git stash` not used**. A PERMANENT test (`splice_mrtr_params_would_delete_a_tasks_update_payload`) now pins the mechanical half: the strip is UNCONDITIONAL, the method gate is upstream in `mrtr_eligible`, so eligibility alone is what would delete the body. `MRTR_METHODS` and the Phase-113 lock test are **byte-identical to HEAD**, verified by extracting each block from `git show HEAD:` and `diff`ing (IDENTICAL) rather than by eyeballing — the only nearby hunk is `@@ -124,0 +125,18 @@`, a pure rustdoc insertion ABOVE the const. **Widening `frame_routing_pair` is inert on the SERVER, verified rather than assumed:** `method_and_name_of` has exactly one consumer (`classify_v2_request`), whose `body_name` feeds `cross_check_name`, which returns `Ok(())` for a non-`logical_name_key` method before comparing anything; the outbound echo uses the HEADER value from `require_three_headers`, not the body name. So server-side enforcement stays OFF — recorded as **D-114-C** (owner: Phase 118), one predicate to flip (`is_name_bearing_method` → `name_bearing_key`) and BREAKING for clients still sending the empty value. The tradeoff is written into `TASK_NAME_BEARING_METHODS`' own rustdoc so a reader of the table cannot miss it. **`tests/v2_tasks_client.rs` (561 lines, 10 tests) uses a raw-TCP capture socket, and the reason is worth carrying:** `tasks/*` is not routed on the v2 wire yet (TASK-03), so a REAL pmcp server would answer `-32601` and the interesting request would never be observed — the capture socket has no gate. It echoes the request id in a canned `-32601` rather than answering `204`, because a `204` left the client blocked on a receive and each test took 6.4 s; with the echo a single test runs in **1.15 s**. Prior-wave context — **114-05 has landed: a tasks-backed server is now DISCOVERABLE by a v2 client, and each era's serialization boundary hides the other era's spelling — including a v1 projection the plan did not have and the wire proved necessary.** `apply_tasks_capability_rule` gained its v2 arm: the SAME `has_backend` fact that sets `capabilities.tasks` now also ensures `capabilities.extensions["io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks"] = {}`, through `entry(..).or_insert_with(..)` — the extensions-map twin of the existing `tasks.is_none()` guard — so **both builder call sites (`builder.rs:1056`, `mod.rs:4770`) reach the new behaviour with ZERO edits**, which is exactly what HTASK-01's "one free fn, two callers, never a re-derived copy" buys, and no existing tasks server needs a code change. **The value is `{}` and only `{}`:** `default_tasks_capability()`'s `list`/`cancel`/`requests.tools.call` flags are deliberately NOT projected in (D-03) — advertising `list: true` on an era where `tasks/list` answers `-32601` is the capability lie the endpoint-backed rule exists to prevent — and both the unit test and live test 1 assert **equality with `{}`**, not presence, because a presence-only assertion passes on precisely the change that would break this. **THE MEASUREMENT THAT CHANGED THE PLAN, and it is the most transferable thing here.** Task 2 instructed "verify by measurement rather than assumption" that v1 `initialize` is unaffected. Measured: the premise is HALF true. `initialize` genuinely does not share `discover_result_from_capabilities` — it builds `InitializeResult` from `self.capabilities.clone()` at three sites (`core.rs:558`, `server/mod.rs:1538`, `wasm_server.rs:129`) — **but it shares the STRUCT, and the build-time rule mutates it.** So "leave the v1 path untouched" did NOT freeze v1 bytes: a v1 `initialize` came back carrying `"extensions":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks":{}}`, read verbatim off a real socket by the new test BEFORE any of this was reasoned about. That is a v1 wire change on **every tasks server that exists today** — the D-02 lock and the T-114-16 `mitigate` — and **`tests/v1_tasks_golden.rs` could never have caught it**, because that suite pins `tasks/*` bodies, not `initialize`. Note also that the plan's own test 5 as literally written ("against the SAME tasks-backed server" = the shared `spawn_tasks_server`) could not have detected it either: that fixture pre-seeds an `extensions` map, so `!raw.contains("extensions")` would have failed for the wrong reason — test 5 therefore builds a fixture with NO configured extensions, which is what makes the raw-string check mean "the auto-advertisement leaked" and nothing else. **The fix (Rule 2 deviation) is `core::project_capabilities_for_v1`, the exact mirror of the v2 projection, and it is VALUE-CONDITIONAL on purpose:** it drops the entry only when its value is exactly what `tasks_extension_value()` writes (compared against the same fn the advertisement uses, so the two cannot drift), because a non-empty value is unambiguously operator-authored and deleting it would be the mirror-image failure of overwriting it — test 6's rustdoc argues an operator's value is the operator's, and the v1 projection honours that rule instead of contradicting it one file away. If the removal empties the map, the map is DROPPED rather than emitted as `"extensions":{}`. **One residual case is stated, not hidden:** an operator who explicitly configured exactly `{}` before this plan loses it from the v1 wire, being indistinguishable by construction from the auto value. Wired at **BOTH** initialize sites (twin-site parity — fixing only `ServerCore` would have left the leak live on the path the tests actually exercise, and `server/mod.rs` is **one file beyond the plan's declared `files_modified`**); `WasmServer` deliberately NOT wired, since the whole task subsystem is `cfg(not(wasm32))` so no wasm build can auto-gain the entry. **Both projections work on a CLONE** — capabilities are per-server, a projection is per-request-era, and mutating the stored struct would make the first v2 discover permanently change what every subsequent v1 client sees; `two successive projections yield identical output` pins it. v2 discover clears `capabilities.tasks` and removes ONLY `experimental.tasks` (D-02 rejected suppressing the whole block; an emptied `experimental` is emitted as `{}` rather than dropped, because omit-empty is a second wire rule this phase does not own). **`V2_TASKS_NOT_NEGOTIATED`'s rustdoc asserted AS FACT that pmcp advertises no tasks entry** — false the moment Task 1 landed (113-29's failure class) — so it was rewritten in the same commit; **the plan's prescribed replacement text was BACKWARDS** (it said the constant means "no task backend configured", but the constant fires on the `(store IS configured, era v2)` row) and the doc now says what is true: the extension IS advertised, what has not landed is the v2 **semantics** (TASK-03). Its message VALUE is left byte-unchanged deliberately — rewording a live refusal from a plan that does not own the route is how two plans come to disagree about one wire string. **A grep trap worth carrying:** the natural replacement sentence "…advertises no extension entry either" would have reintroduced the exact phrase the plan's acceptance grep bans; it was rephrased to "carries no". Check an acceptance grep against the NEW text before committing. `tests/v2_tasks_negotiation.rs` (416 lines, 6 tests) drives real frames over the shared harness — **extended, never forked** (`git diff tests/common/v2.rs` empty) — with absence asserted as key ABSENCE (both fields carry `skip_serializing_if`, so a value check would accept the exact falsy shape a regression emits) and **the non-vacuity half firing first** (test 2's server is built WITH `experimental.tasks`, because against the shared harness — which has no `experimental` map at all — that assertion would have passed against a projection that suppressed nothing). **THREE orthogonal negative controls, all reverted with `shasum` equal before/after, `git stash` NOT used:** NC-1 (disable the extensions insert) fails tests **1 and 6 only**; NC-2 (disable the v2 v1-key suppression) fails test **2 alone**; NC-3 (disable the v1 projection) fails test **5 plus its 2 unit tests** while all five v2 rows stay green — and NC-3 shows `v1_projection_preserves_an_operator_configured_...` correctly still PASSING, since it guards against over-removal, not for removal. `make quality-gate` **exit 0**; clean re-runs through `/usr/bin/make` gave `test-all` **170 result lines / 2777 passed / 0 failed** and `validate-always` **86 / 1695 / 0**, both with **zero truncation markers**. **Two counting caveats stated rather than papered over:** (a) **the gate's own log is not a measurement** — RTK truncated it to 611 lines with `... (6347 lines truncated)` and every `test result:` line fell inside the truncated region, so `grep -c '^test result:'` returned **0 on a run that passed**; the EXIT CODE is the authoritative signal; (b) **256 lines / 4472 passed is NOT comparable to 114-04's 258 / 4576** and no reconciliation is claimed — that figure came from the full aggregate, which additionally runs `pmcp-package-gate` over the workspace-excluded `pmcp-package` crate, a component not re-run here; the delta (2 lines, 104 tests) is consistent with that being the whole difference but was not measured. `make lint` exit 0 twice (after Task 1 and after Task 2); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` exit 0; `cargo semver-checks --baseline-rev 27364eb1` **223/223 "no semver update required"**; `pmat analyze complexity` — **and its output shape is a trap: `violations` lives under `summary`, NOT at top level**, so a naive top-level query returns "0 violations" **vacuously**; queried correctly it is 4 violations, **0 in `src/`**, 0 in either changed file (all four are test files in `crates/`, and `src/` being clean means **D-113-U's `write_canonical` no longer appears** — observed, not investigated).
