No contract YAML (114-20 waiver); no `tasks/update` row in `MRTR_METHODS`; row 23 still 114-10's. **The advertisement and the semantics are now deliberately out of step:** a tasks-backed v2 server advertises the extension while `tasks/result` still answers `-32601` on v2 — that gap is TASK-03's, documented at the constant, and whoever closes it must replace the constant's value AND its match row together. **NEXT: 114-06** (client negotiation — its `assert_capability` v2 arm should read `extensions[TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY]`, the constant not a literal, and expect `{}`), **114-07**, and **114-10** before any v2 `tasks/get` dependency. *(superseded as the current entry — see above; 114-06 has landed.)*

Prior-wave context — **114-04 has landed: `tasks/update` can now reach a backend, and the SERVER-recorded input kinds are readable without reaching the private record.** Five additive defaulted `TaskStore` methods (`deliver_task_inputs`, `task_input_snapshot`, `record_input_requests`, `set_error`, `get_error`), the `supports_inputs()` probe (`false` by default, `supports_results()`'s shape line for line), and one defaulted `TaskRouter::handle_tasks_update` — **`+1254/-28` across exactly the two declared files, `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` byte-unchanged (T-114-SC), zero packages installed.** **The seam cross-AI review flagged as missing was re-verified on disk, not taken on trust:** `TaskStore::get` returns only the wire `Task` and `TaskRecord` is a PRIVATE struct, so items (1)-(4) alone left `task_dispatch.rs` with no way to read the kinds 114-14's `decode_for` must decode against nor the `inputRequests` 114-11 must inline; `task_input_snapshot` + `TaskInputSnapshot::kind_of()` is that path, and a key the server never issued returns `None` (asserted). **The single most reusable measurement: `TaskStatus::can_transition_to(&Working)` IS the awaiting-input predicate.** It is true for `InputRequired` **ALONE** — `Working → Working` is a rejected self-transition and all three terminals reject outright — so one call to the shared, 46-transition-test-pinned state machine expresses the whole rule with no second predicate to drift, and `record_input_requests` gets its terminal refusal from the same call in the other direction. **`TaskInputDelivery` is a VALUE, not `Ok(())`**, because accepted / ignored / complete must stay distinguishable or 114-14 has to re-read the record to recover partial-vs-complete; a partial delivery persists its responses and the task **STAYS `input_required`**, and the transition additionally requires a **non-empty `accepted` set**, so a delivery that changed nothing cannot resume a paused task (vacuous completeness would have let a client resume with keys the server never issued). **Two plan-text ambiguities were resolved rather than papered over:** `TaskInputSnapshot::input_requests` carries the **FULL** recorded set (what 114-11 inlines, inventory row 23) with `outstanding()` as the derived unanswered subset, since the plan's "the outstanding `InputRequests`" is wanted two different ways by its two consumers; and `record_input_requests` records **ONE round per task** in the in-crate store, refusing rather than overwriting, which makes "a second call erases delivered answers" **unreachable by construction** instead of merely tested against (114-07's `GenericTaskStore` may relax to supersede-with-merge — said in the rustdoc). Neither new public struct is `#[non_exhaustive]`: both are **RETURNED** by methods out-of-tree stores override, so that attribute would make an out-of-tree `deliver_task_inputs` impossible to write — reason recorded in the type's own rustdoc so it is not "corrected" later. `Some(empty)` on `input_requests` is preserved as distinct from `None` ("a round that asked for nothing"), echoing 113-27's `Option`-vs-empty-map discipline. **The bounds division of responsibility is written at BOTH ends and NAMED BY ROLE with the count stated — "Four, not five"** — so a future reader cannot add a fifth check here by miscounting `MAX_REQUEST_STATE_LEN` in (it bounds the MRTR token; `tasks/update` carries none). **Additivity is a COMPILE-TIME proof, not an inspection:** the test module's `DefaultOnlyStore` implements only the pre-114 required methods and overrides none of the seven additions, so any addition made required would fail `E0046` — its comment now says exactly that so the guard is not deleted as dead weight — and `default_impl_store_reports_inputs_unsupported` asserts every default is honest (`Internal` for the three writes, `NotFound` for the two reads). **Three deviations, all recorded:** *(Rule 3)* a two-commit-lived `#[allow(dead_code)]` on the three new `TaskRecord` fields, because the plan's own task split leaves them write-only for one commit and `RUSTFLAGS = -D warnings` makes that a **hard error** — removed in Task 2, `grep` at HEAD returns 0; *(Rule 1)* two `iter_on_single_items` clippy **errors** that `cargo build` and `cargo nextest` were both green on — **fifth consecutive plan where `make lint` caught what a weaker command would not**; *(Rule 1)* a **1 ms-TTL setup race** in my new expiry test **and in the pre-existing `cleanup_expired_drops_result`** — every `InMemoryTaskStore` write goes through `validate_access`, which returns `Expired` (not a lost write) once the TTL elapsed, so setup loses to the clock under load. Diagnosis was **confirmed by measurement, not inferred**: after hardening mine, run 1 of a 5-run repeat then failed the *pre-existing* one; both are now 500 ms with the reason at the site, and deliberately a `let` **not a `const`** so the plan's "no new numeric constant" acceptance grep stays honest — **6 consecutive** clean runs after. `make quality-gate` **exit 0 — 258 test-result lines, all `ok.`, 4576 passed, 0 failed**, and the arithmetic corroborates the change exactly: 114-03's 4534 + (18 new lib tests × 2, the gate counts them in both `test-unit` and `validate-always`) + 6 new doctests = **4576**, with **no new test binary**, and both inputs verified independently (`task_store.rs` 43 → 61 tests; doctest fences in that file 8 → 20). `make lint` exit 0; `make wasm-build` exit 0 at the **inherited 86** warnings with **0** naming either changed file (both modules are `#[cfg(not(wasm32))]`); `cargo check -p pmcp-tasks` exit 0 with a **zero-byte diff** to `crates/pmcp-tasks/`; `tests/v1_tasks_golden.rs` green inside 60/60 on `test(/tasks/)`, so **v1 bytes did not move**; a function-name set-difference against `27364eb1` reports **REMOVED: (none)** — the 28 deletion lines are hunk realignment plus the three intended edits, not lost code. Semver was **isolated**: `--baseline-rev 27364eb1` is **223/223 "no semver update required"**, confirming a defaulted method on a public non-sealed trait is non-breaking; the crates.io baseline's `type_marked_deprecated` on `OptimizedSseTransport` remains **inherited from `9b33a00f`**. **NEW process note, and it inverts a previous one:** `git diff | grep -c '^-[^-]'` returned **0** through the RTK proxy when the true count was **28** — so an "insertions-only" claim made that way is not a measurement; redo it with `/usr/bin/git`. `make quality-gate` again exceeded a 10-minute foreground window (SIGTERM/143) and completed only when launched **fully detached with an exit-code marker file** and polled. **`git stash` was not used at any point.** **`.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` is untouched (0-byte diff) — TASK-02/TASK-06 are IMPLEMENTED at the store/router layer but stay `[~]` and `requirements mark-complete` was deliberately NOT run, because `114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` flips TASK-01..06 as a GROUP and only on a `PUBLISHED-CONFIRMED` landing, and `## Verdict` is still `PENDING`.** No contract YAML (114-20's option-b waiver); no `tasks/update` row added to `MRTR_METHODS` (row 34's `splice_mrtr_params` trap is 114-06/114-14's to avoid); **row 23 was NOT designed around** — this plan supplies the storage and leaves `own_reserved_result_fields`' silent deletion of `inputRequests` to **114-10**, which must land before anything depends on a v2 `tasks/get`. `supports_inputs()` has **no caller in `src/`** — this plan dispatches nothing, by design. New: `.planning/phases/114-tasks-extension-migration/deferred-items.md` records **D-114-A** (a `v2_error_envelope` test failed once with the macOS keychain `ioErr -36` signature at the pre-existing `.expect` in `src/shared/streamable_http.rs:458`; passes in isolation, 136/136 on re-run, and **`df -h /` showed 19 GiB free — so this is NOT the known disk-exhaustion mode that produces the identical signature**, worth knowing before a future bisect chases the wrong cause) and **D-114-B** (the 1 ms-TTL shape, fixed here, may exist elsewhere). Also corrected for later plans: `gsd-sdk query state.record-metric` takes **named** args (`--phase --plan --duration --tasks --files`), not the positional form the executor prompt shows. **NEXT: wave 2 — 114-05** (server negotiation), **114-06** (client negotiation), **114-07** (`GenericTaskStore<B>` input delivery), plus **114-10** (row 23) before any v2 `tasks/get` dependency; **D-113-U** (`write_canonical` cognitive 26 vs. the PR-blocking PMAT cap of 25) is still open and unowned and this plan did not touch that file.

Prior-wave context — **114-03 has landed: a client can now declare an extension at all, and the tasks extension has exactly one key spelling and one `{}`-serializing type.** Research gap **F6** is closed — `ClientCapabilities` had no `extensions` field, so a client's declaration was **silently dropped by serde**, which made D-04's client gate, DQ1's v2 create trigger and DQ3's `-32021` refusal literally unimplementable. The field is `Option<HashMap<String, serde_json::Value>>` with `skip_serializing_if`, **appended after `experimental`** so no pre-existing key moves in the serialized order (serde emits in declaration order and this crate builds `serde_json` with `preserve_order`). Its rustdoc carries the two facts a reader cannot infer from the type: on **v2 the declaration travels PER REQUEST** inside `_meta["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities"]` because v2 is stateless and has no handshake — so the field is both the type the client's per-request `_meta` emission serializes FROM and the type the server's already-resolved `ProtocolContext::client_capabilities` deserializes INTO — and the **T-114-07 mitigation in prose**: the map is self-reported and forgeable, says only what the client SUPPORTS, may be read to decide whether a capability may be SERVED, and must never be read as identity. `TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY = "io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks"` lands under a `# Provenance` heading naming `schema/vendored/ext-tasks/schema.ts` at 114-01's pinned commit `2c1425d9…`, the D-18 hold, and the **independent core-spec corroboration** — with the measured correction that `schema/draft/examples/ServerCapabilities/extensions-tasks.json` is **NOT in this repo** (it lives in `modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol`, was read at `main`, and is cited as corroboration rather than a pinned artifact, so nobody hunts for it on disk or assumes the provenance tripwire covers it). `TasksExtensionCapability` is a **zero-field braced** struct (`{}` on the wire, not `null` — a unit struct would have serialized as `null`), `#[non_exhaustive]`, whose rustdoc records that upstream types it `Record<string, never>`, i.e. a future field is *possible* but should not be expected, and any field added later MUST be `Option<_> + skip_serializing_if` to keep the `{}` form. **The negative control is where the evidence is.** Removing `skip_serializing_if` from the new field failed `client_default_serializes_without_extensions_key` with `got: {"extensions":null}` — **the exact falsy value a value-based assertion would have accepted**, so "assert key ABSENCE on the serialized string" is now a measurement, not a style preference; and the control was **ORTHOGONAL** (the other four new tests all still PASSED), so it attributes to the one test that owns the property. Reverted from a byte-for-byte backup (`shasum` equal before/after); **`git stash` was not used**. **One deviation, Rule 3:** `ClientCapabilities::full()` builds the struct with an **exhaustive literal** and broke with `E0063`; it is set to `extensions: None` with the reason in a source comment, because declaring an Extensions-Track capability there would have changed the `initialize` bytes of every existing caller (D-02) — precisely the fix that looks "more complete" and is wrong. **Semver was proven the isolating way rather than argued:** `cargo semver-checks check-release --baseline-rev 27364eb1` is **223/223, "no semver update required"**, and `cargo public-api diff 27364eb1..HEAD` reports **Removed: (none)** and **Changed: (none)**. Against the crates.io baseline the branch instead reports **222/1 — `type_marked_deprecated` on `OptimizedSseTransport`**, which commit `9b33a00f` (113.1-03) introduced and which is present at this plan's start commit: **inherited, not caused here**, and later plans should isolate with `--baseline-rev` instead of adopting it. The whole plan diff is **insertions-only in one file** (`+343`, `grep -c '^-[^-]'` → **0**), so D-02's pre-existing `ServerCapabilities` lock is provably byte-unchanged; `tests/v1_tasks_golden.rs` (14) and `tests/vendored_schema_provenance.rs` (5) both ran green inside `cargo test --test '*'`. `make quality-gate` **exit 0 — 258 test-result lines, 4534 passed, 0 failed**, and the arithmetic corroborates the change: 114-02's baseline was the same 258 lines at 4522, plus 5 lib tests counted twice by the gate (`test-unit` and `validate-always`) plus 2 new doctests = **exactly 4534**, with no new test binary. `cargo doc` warnings 29 → **0 naming `capabilities.rs`**; wasm green at the inherited 86 warnings, **0** naming that file; `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` byte-unchanged. **Two process notes worth carrying:** the aggregate `make quality-gate` was **killed twice by the environment mid-run** (no exit marker, no live processes, 26 GiB free and 85% memory free — NOT the known disk-exhaustion mode) and only completed when launched detached **and** waited for inside the same Bash invocation; and **RTK truncated a redirected log to a `... (1622 lines truncated)` marker with the `test result: ok` line filtered out**, so every later measurement used absolute binary paths — a truncated log is not a green log. **`.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` is untouched (0-byte diff) — TASK-01 is IMPLEMENTED but stays `[~]` and `requirements mark-complete` was deliberately NOT run, because `114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` flips TASK-01..06 as a GROUP and only on a `PUBLISHED-CONFIRMED` landing, and `## Verdict` is still `PENDING`.** A grep for `TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY` outside `src/types/capabilities.rs` returns **0 files** — this plan negotiates nothing, by design. **NEXT: 114-04** (`src/server/task_store.rs` + `src/server/tasks.rs`, same wave, untouched by this plan), with 114-05 (server half) and 114-06 (client half) now unblocked on the types; 114-06 should replace its literal extension keys with `TASKS_EXTENSION_KEY` when it wires `v2_body_with_client_extensions`. *(superseded — see the current entry above; 114-04 has landed.)*

Prior-wave context — **114-02 has landed: today's v1 `tasks/*` response bytes are pinned on BOTH backend paths, and the comparison was proven load-bearing rather than assumed.** `tests/v1_tasks_golden.rs` (1012 lines) drives six v1 surfaces — `tasks/get` on `working` and on `completed`, `tasks/result` while pending, `tasks/list`, `tasks/cancel`, and the task-augmented `tools/call` create envelope — as REAL loopback HTTP requests against a **not-opted-in** server, each run **twice**: once `InMemoryTaskStore`-backed, once backed by a local test `TaskRouter`. That doubling is the point: D-11's claim is that the v2 reshape sits ABOVE the `serde_json::Value` `TaskRouter` seam, and a suite exercising only the store could not detect a v1 byte change on the path DynamoDB and Redis deployments actually use. **`assert_v1_bytes` compares RAW BYTES, not parsed JSON**, after replacing the three genuinely per-run values (the UUID `taskId` and two RFC-3339 timestamps) with placeholders — and the substitution is proven non-destructive on every call by a **same-width length invariant** plus an unchanged **per-key occurrence count**, so "it never deletes a key" is a checked property, not a comment. Timestamps are **NOT** fixed width (measured: both `.360` and `.358382` appeared in one probe run, because `chrono`'s `to_rfc3339` emits 0/3/6/9 fractional digits), which is exactly why the width-preserving pass pads the token and the golden-comparison pass uses a bare one. **The negative controls are where the evidence is.** NC-1 swapped `Task::status` and `Task::ttl` in the serde declaration: five store-backed fixtures FAILED on the raw comparison. **Its supplement is the load-bearing measurement** — with the reorder still in place and only the raw comparison disabled, **all 14 tests PASSED**, because this crate builds `serde_json` with `preserve_order` so `Map` is an `IndexMap` whose `PartialEq` is order-INDEPENDENT. A structural-only golden suite is therefore blind to a v1 key reorder, measured rather than argued. NC-2 added `skip_serializing_if` to `Task::ttl`: four fixtures FAILED and `get_terminal` correctly kept PASSING (its `ttl` is `Some(60000)`) — but its supplement showed the **structural** comparison also catches that one, so NC-2 is recorded as proving the omission-vs-null property *pinned*, NOT as a second proof that the raw compare is what catches it. NC-2 is only non-vacuous because the store fixture sets `default_ttl_ms: None`, putting a real `"ttl":null` on the wire — `ttl` is `Task`'s ONLY `Option` field without `skip_serializing_if`. **The `_meta` asymmetry was honoured, not copy-pasted:** `build_task_created_response` deliberately emits `_meta.relatedTask` on the v1 create envelope, so a blanket `!raw.contains("_meta")` would fail for the wrong reason; `_meta` is asserted ABSENT on get/list/cancel/result and PRESENT with **exactly** the `io.modelcontextprotocol/related-task` key on create. **Three legitimate backend differences are named at their fixtures so a later diff is attributable:** router-backed `tasks/result` answers `-32603` (the router fall-through returns before the frozen `-32002` branch); a router-ONLY high-level `Server` **cannot mint a task from `tools/call`** at all, because `maybe_build_task_created` requires a `TaskStore`, so that call falls through to an ordinary `CallToolResult` — both worth knowing before 114-05/114-10 gate those paths; and the router goldens normalize NOTHING, so they are pinned verbatim. **The shared harness was EXTENDED, never forked** (`git diff tests/common/v2.rs` has **zero deletion lines**): `OptionalBearer` was **MOVED** out of `tests/v2_subscriptions.rs` (`grep -c "struct OptionalBearer"` there is now **0**) because two definitions of "this server admits anonymous callers" is how a security test comes to pass for the wrong reason; `spawn_tasks_server` requires an **explicit** `AuthPosture` (`None`/`Optional`/`Required`, no `Default`) so a security test cannot pass by silently getting a no-auth server (T-114-05), and `AuthPosture::Optional` is the ONLY way a test reaches the `(None, has_auth_provider = true)` row TASK-05's refusal branch lives on; plus `v2_body_with_client_extensions` (additive sibling of an untouched `v2_body_with_caps`, extension keys caller-supplied so it has **no** dependency on 114-03), `tasks_request_body`, and one `teardown(handle, sockets)` order for D-113-T. Two plan-text corrections were needed and are recorded: `TaskRouterImpl` lives in `crates/pmcp-tasks`, which is **not** a root dev-dependency, so using it would have edited `Cargo.toml` against the plan's own `T-114-SC` byte-unchanged constraint (a local `GoldenRouter` is used, and constant router bytes are the sharper assertion anyway); and the plan's acceptance grep `assert_eq!(normalized` could not match a **rustfmt-formatted** macro, so the failure text moved into `wire_break_message()` to keep the byte comparison on one greppable line. `make quality-gate` **exit 0 (258 test-result lines, 4522 passed, 0 failed)**; `make lint` exit 0 (it again caught two lints a bare `-D warnings` run misses: `doc_markdown`, `needless_pass_by_value`); all 10 harness-consuming binaries green (181 passed) — note they must be selected by `binary_id(..)`, since nextest's `test()` predicate matches test NAMES and every golden test carries a `v1_tasks_golden` prefix for that reason. `git diff --stat -- src/ Cargo.toml Cargo.lock` **empty**. **`.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` is untouched (0-byte diff) — TASK-05/TASK-06 stay unflipped and `requirements mark-complete` was deliberately NOT run: `114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` flips TASK-01..06 as a GROUP and only on a `PUBLISHED-CONFIRMED` landing, and `## Verdict` is still `PENDING`.** **NEXT: 114-03** (`src/types/capabilities.rs` — the typed tasks extension capability; it also lands the `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks` constant that `v2_body_with_client_extensions` callers should then pass instead of a literal). *(superseded — see the current entry above; 114-03 has landed.)*

Prior-wave context — **114-01 has landed: the v2 tasks wire schema is no longer a network finding.** `modelcontextprotocol/ext-tasks` `schema/draft/{schema.ts,schema.json}` are vendored at pinned commit **`2c1425d9a288b9b1f489430fe1e00bb392b47e48`** (2026-07-15) under `schema/vendored/ext-tasks/`, byte-for-byte, fetched over HTTPS **at the SHA and never at `main`**. The measurements match `114-RESEARCH.md` exactly (374 lines / 9421 bytes; 46,903 bytes), which independently corroborates that research read the artifact this plan pinned. **Byte-identity is proven TWICE, not asserted once:** SHA-256 proves the files have not changed *since* the fetch but cannot prove the fetch was faithful, so the git blob SHA-1s were cross-checked against the GitHub contents API at the pinned commit (`2634c47c…` / `d6ccaff7…`, both exact, sizes matching). `PROVENANCE.md` carries repo, SHA, dates, per-file size + SHA-256, the upstream `Status: Experimental` description, a reproduce-from-this-file-alone recipe and a change protocol, and states plainly that these files are a **read-only reference artifact** — never compiled, never read by the build, zero dependency edges. **`Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` are byte-unchanged**, and `schema/` was deliberately left OUT of `[package] exclude`: 56 KB is immaterial and excluding it would break the tripwire for a downstream `cargo test` on the published crate, the exact failure that forced `tests/team_contracts_conformance.rs` out when `contracts/` was excluded. **`tests/vendored_schema_provenance.rs` (5 tests) makes an unrecorded edit a test failure**, discovering files with `read_dir` recursively at runtime so a newly vendored file is in scope automatically and cannot be added un-recorded; it asserts **attribution only**, never schema content. **Five negative controls were run and reverted, and the orthogonality is the evidence:** an appended byte fires FAILURE MODE 1 naming the file; deleting `schema.json`'s recorded digest fires mode 1 **alone** (mode 3 passes); removing `PROVENANCE.md` fires mode 2 on four tests while the anti-vacuity test correctly still passes; removing `schema.json` fires mode 3 **alone and by name** (mode 1 passes), proving mode 3 is not a shadow of mode 1. **The fifth is the load-bearing one and it corrected the plan:** the plan's own acceptance command `grep -Eo '[0-9a-f]{40}'` **passes vacuously** — a 64-char SHA-256 contains a 40-char prefix — so with every real 40-hex pin stripped that grep still returned two matches while the test correctly FAILED. The test therefore counts **MAXIMAL** hex runs of exactly 40, and the reason is written into its own failure message. Two further plan gaps were closed on the way: `sha256sum` is a fallback digest binary so the tripwire cannot silently skip on a CI image without perl `shasum` (and the skip prints `SKIPPED (ASSERTED NOTHING)`), and all five tests were renamed to carry a `vendored_schema_` prefix because nextest's `test()` predicate matches **test names**, not binaries — as written the plan's verify command reported `Starting 0 tests` and `error: no tests to run`. `make lint` again caught what a bare clippy run would not (`needless_collect`), and control 5 was **re-run after that refactor** and still fired. **`114-SPEC-RECHECK.md` records the D-18 hold** mirroring 113's section names so the two diff: `## Verdict` is **`PENDING`**; the **DQ6 amendment** makes the trigger **BOTH** repos — a versioned (non-`draft`) directory must exist in `modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol` **AND** `ext-tasks`, because 113's condition was written before tasks moved out of the core spec and six `[~]` requirements must not flip on a core-only publication event; **partial publication lands in `STILL-ABSENT`, not a fourth state**; and a **39-row Wire-Value Inventory** names, for every value the next 17 plans write, the file it lands in and the plan that owns it. Measured on **2026-07-28, the date the final spec was due**: core holds `2024-11-05`/`2025-03-26`/`2025-06-18`/`2025-11-25`/`draft` and **no `2026-07-28`**; **ext-tasks holds only `draft` and has never published a versioned directory at all**. Two inventory rows are flagged for their owners: **row 23** (`inputRequests` required on `InputRequiredTask`) is the highest-severity — Phase 113's `own_reserved_result_fields` silently deletes that key, owned by **114-10 (DQ2)**, which must land before any plan depends on v2 `tasks/get`; and **row 34** (`Mcp-Name` = `params.taskId`) must use a **separate** name-key table, since `logical_name_key` and `mrtr_eligible` both derive from `MRTR_METHODS` and a row there would make `tasks/update` MRTR-eligible so `splice_mrtr_params` would delete its whole payload (**114-06, DQ4**). The **`-32003`-vs-`-32021` upstream disagreement** is its own inventory row with a direction-recheck obligation, resolved for now as DQ3 (both codes, two meanings; **no new wire value minted**). `make fmt-check` and `make lint` exit 0; `git diff --stat -- Cargo.toml Cargo.lock` empty. **`.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` is untouched (0-byte diff) — TASK-01..06 stay unflipped and `requirements mark-complete` was deliberately NOT run, because flipping them would contradict the hold this plan exists to record.** **NEXT: 114-02** (the v1 byte-identity baseline and the shared test harness the rest of the phase depends on). *(superseded — see the current entry above; 114-02 has landed.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-28 landed and the phase's open question became a RECORDED DECISION: `hold`.** `113-SPEC-RECHECK.md`'s binding re-verification obligation had exactly two landing states, `PUBLISHED-CONFIRMED` and `PUBLISHED-DRIFT`, and both presuppose publication — so the outcome the evidence says is most likely, "still absent", had nowhere to land and the eleven `[~]` requirements would have stayed `[~]` **by default rather than by choice**. Step 4 now has **three** branches; the third is **`STILL-ABSENT`**, an explicitly legitimate, non-failing outcome governed by the new `## Third Outcome Policy` (decided by Guy Ernest, 2026-07-27, at the 113-28 Task 2 blocking checkpoint; **no conditions, review date or scope narrowing were stated** and none were invented). Under `hold`: `## Verdict` stays `PENDING`, HTTP-01..08 / CLNT-01/02/05 stay `[~]`, the obligation is NOT discharged and rolls forward, the run is still RECORDED so "we checked and it was absent" stays distinguishable from "nobody checked", and **arm 2 is run regardless** — it is not publication-gated. **The TRIGGER is restated as a CONDITION, not a date** (addendum Finding 10): the gate becomes runnable when *a versioned schema directory exists*, so it can be neither treated as due nor as discharged merely because a day passed; the three superseded "on or after 2026-07-28" instructions in that file now say so at each site. **The decision was made on evidence re-measured that hour, not inherited** (probe 2026-07-27T14:17:03Z–14:25:05Z, `gh` 2.64.0, every exit 0, nothing recorded UNAVAILABLE). **The single most useful NEW fact — `.github/workflows/cut-release.yml`:** `kind=final` is a **`workflow_dispatch`** running `cp -r schema/draft "schema/$VERSION"` plus one `sed`-stamped `LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION`, then opening a reviewed PR — so **the absence of an in-flight commit is the EXPECTED state, not a signal**, and a dispatch today would publish **our exact `-32020`/`-32021`/`-32022`**. Finding 7 re-verified against a FRESHER `main` HEAD (`31eefec6`, 32 commits past our pin): `schema.ts`/`schema.json` still blob- AND sha256-identical — **zero drift for 11 days**. All **82** open PRs enumerated: **11** modify `schema/draft/schema.ts`, **0** touch the `-3202x` block; **PR #2678** (proposing `-32000`/`-32001`/`-32002` in the adjacent implementation-defined range, contradicting the `-32002`-reserved rule 113-29 just era-gated against) is the one forward risk to re-check at every future run. Finding 9 CORRECTED: PR #3006 is a TypeScript interface rename plus a response wrapper and touches **neither** the `subscriptionId` wire key **nor** `NotificationMetaObject`. **`prose: correct` was answered too — both of 113-32's routed corrections are AUTHORISED and deliberately NOT APPLIED** (`stateless.ts:988-1015` → `983-1016`, and naming `resources.subscribe` where HTTP-08 describes what gates the stream); they are recorded as *authorised for the re-verification run* so every requirement-text change in this phase lands in one reviewable place. **HTTP-07's wording is explicitly NOT in that table** — 113-23 measured it correct. Round gate re-run whole: `make quality-gate` exit 0 (**252 result lines, 4487 passed, 0 failed**), `make lint` exit 0 zero warnings, `semver-checks` **223/223 no update required**. **FOUND: D-113-U** — the PR-blocking PMAT gate is at **3** violations, up from 2: `write_canonical` cognitive **26**, introduced by 113-26, measured at **0** in that file at the pre-113-26 baseline. Unowned, not fixed (this plan changed no source file), and it **needs an owner before this branch merges**. `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` **untouched (0-byte diff)**, no checkbox flipped, `## Verdict` still `PENDING`. **NEXT: the re-verification run — when a versioned schema directory exists — executing BOTH arms under the now-three-branch procedure; and an owner for D-113-U, D-113-R (still blocks HTTP-09 substantively), D-113-Q, D-113-S, D-113-T.**

Prior-wave context — **113-29 has landed: addendum Finding 11 is closed by EXECUTION — both of pmcp's `-32002` emission sites were v2-reachable, and neither by the route its source comment implied.** The draft spec's § Error Codes (added AFTER the `2026-07-28-RC` tag) says protocol version 2026-07-28 **MUST NOT emit** `-32002` or `-32042`. pmcp had two `-32002` sites, both commented v1-scoped, neither ever traced. Driving a real v2 request at each read `-32002` off both responses: `src/server/core.rs`'s server-not-initialized gate answers a v2 `tools/call` through the **PUBLIC** `ProtocolHandler` trait — a surface the streamable-HTTP era gating Phase 113 built never covered — and `src/server/task_dispatch.rs`'s `tasks/result` pending refusal answers a real v2 HTTP request. **The second one is the finding that matters for future verification:** the plan prescribed an in-process `ServerCore` probe for it, and on that surface the site reports UNREACHABLE, because `ClientRequest::TasksResult` is an enumerated non-`_meta`-bearing variant with nowhere to put an era signal. The era arrives elsewhere — `run_v2_header_gate` reads `params._meta` off the **RAW body** before deserialization — so a trace that stopped at the typed path would have recorded "v1-only by construction" and shipped the violation. Both sites are now era-gated behind named predicates following 113-04's `sessions_active_for` shape: `v1_initialize_gate_applies` **skips** the gate on v2 (a v2 request carries no `initialize` handshake by design, HTTP-01, so a different constant would still refuse a conformant request; `stateless_mode` could never have covered it — the builder resolves it by ENVIRONMENT auto-detection), and `is_v1_task_era` routes the v2 `tasks/result` branch to the **existing** `METHOD_NOT_FOUND` with a truthful reason — the tasks extension is not in pmcp's advertised `capabilities.extensions` map — so **no new wire value was invented**. That predicate gates ONLY the `-32002` emission; `tasks/get|list|cancel` stay unchanged, owned by Phase 114 / TASK-03. Each guard is proven load-bearing by a recorded removal run, and **orthogonally**: disabling site A's fails only site A's probe, site B's only site B's, with every v1 negative control GREEN in both. `tests/v2_prohibited_error_codes.rs` (18 tests) carries the executed probes, the two v1 controls, the justified `-32000`..`-32019` SHOULD-NOT inventory (`RATE_LIMITED` kept per 113-18), the measured fact that `UNSUPPORTED_CAPABILITY` has **zero** emission sites, and a source tripwire whose allowlist names the era guard each `V1_TASK_PENDING` site carries — an unlisted site, a deleted guard or a stale entry all fail. `pending_tasks_result_preserves_minus_32002` is GREEN and unedited; `make quality-gate` exit 0 (**252 test-result lines, 4487 passed, 0 failed**); semver-checks 223/223, no update required; **zero** new wasm warnings (85 → 85). `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched — HTTP-01 stays `[~]`. **Next: 113-28, the decision checkpoint.** *(superseded — see the current entry above; 113-28 has landed and the decision is recorded.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-27 has landed: D-113-O is closed — the server no longer types the client's answers by guessing at overlapping shapes.** `src/types/mrtr.rs:987` decoded every `inputResponses` entry with `try_from_value_untagged` (Roots, then Sampling, then Elicitation, first that fits), because at parse time ingress has not opened `requestState` and so cannot know which key maps to which kind. `ElicitResult` and `CreateMessageResult` OVERLAP, Sampling is tried first, and the kind-directed guarantee of T-113-46 therefore held **only on the client**. **The defect was reproduced against the unfixed tree BEFORE any source change**, so the negative control later had a measurement rather than a prediction: at the unit level the answer to an elicitation under `"k"` typed as `Sampling`; end to end over a real socket the handler re-elicited **16 times, 16 handler invocations**, dying on a MISLEADING `-32602 "this request exceeded the server's multi-round-trip round limit"`. **That 16 is plan 113-24's `MAX_MRTR_ROUNDS`, and the composition is now recorded rather than assumed: the ceiling bounds a loop it cannot diagnose, this plan removes the cause, and the negative control shows 113-24's round-limit test staying GREEN while all three D-113-O tests fail** — direct evidence the two fixes are independent. **The server DID know the kinds — it minted them; they simply never travelled.** They now ride inside the AEAD-sealed `Continuation` (`kinds`), built at mint time from the handler's OWN `inputRequests` via `InputRequest::kind()`, never from anything on the request; `MrtrIngest::apply` re-decodes with `decode_for` on the `Verdict::Ok` arm **only** — strictly after the tag check, so the kinds enforced against can be neither chosen nor altered by the client — and before the handler is invoked. **MEASURED CORRECTION to the plan's premise, recorded rather than papered over:** the literal D-113-O answer is **NOT rejected**. `ElicitResult` carries no `deny_unknown_fields` and its `content` is `Option<HashMap<String, Value>>`, so `{"action","content","model"}` **is a valid `ElicitResult`** — the client's answer was well formed all along and the SERVER's guess was the defect. Kind-directed it types as `Elicitation`, the handler's arm matches, and the round **COMPLETES on the first resend with the handler run exactly twice**. Rejection is the outcome for an answer that genuinely cannot be the requested kind (drop `action` and it is a `CreateMessageResult` and nothing else) and for a key the continuation never requested; **both branches are pinned at unit and socket level**. Bending the implementation to make the plan's assertion true would have meant rejecting well-formed client answers — strictly worse than the defect. **`Option<InputRequestKinds>`, NOT an empty-map sentinel:** ABSENT means "minted by a pre-D-113-O build" and DEGRADES to untagged (the rolling-deploy path — not a bypass, since only a holder of the server's key can mint a continuation at all), while `Some(empty)` means "this round asked for NOTHING" and rejects every answer. The plan's empty-means-degrade rule conflated two states, and the second is REACHABLE: a handler may signal `input_required` with an empty `inputRequests`. Pinned against the serialized BYTES an older build sealed, not a hand-built struct. **What a refusal may NAME is decided by PROVENANCE, not convenience:** a `KindMismatch` names its key — taken via `kinds.get_key_value` so the rendered key provably comes out of the SEALED map, server-assigned and bounded by `MAX_REQUEST_STATE_LEN` — while an `Unsolicited` key is CLIENT-chosen by definition, bounded only by the 256 KiB `inputResponses` total, and is carried for programmatic use but **never rendered** (echoing it would both amplify and poison logs — `MrtrParseError`'s existing `Display` discipline). Neither ever renders a VALUE, and the socket test asserts the client-chosen key appears nowhere in the response bytes. **`mint` takes the kinds as an EXPLICIT parameter** so every mint site must decide, mirroring 113-26's only-constructor discipline; `testing::mint_request_state` passes `None` and its public signature is byte-unchanged. **`RequestHandlerExtra::input_responses()` is byte-unchanged for handlers** — only the CORRECTNESS of the typing moves, which is exactly why the fix re-decodes into the same typed map instead of handing handlers raw JSON. **BLAST RADIUS, stated plainly:** this rejects requests that previously succeeded AND completes requests that previously looped, but only on v2 + codec-configured connections presenting a `requestState` that VERIFIES, and only when the answer mismatches its requested kind or names an unrequested key. **No existing test or example relied on the untagged fallback** — 4469 passed / 0 failed with nothing modified to accommodate the change — and the reason is structural: `pmcp::Client` builds its `inputResponses` from the very `inputRequests` the server sent, through `decode_for`, so **a pmcp client is incapable of producing a mismatched answer**. That is also why no test involving one could ever have caught D-113-O, and why every test here drives RAW frames. Raw retention is bounded (256 KiB, inheriting all four pre-existing ingress bounds, which still fire FIRST and are asserted to). Token size MEASURED at **2360 bytes of the 8192 bound** for 64 kinds entries; no new mint guard needed since `mint` already refuses to emit a token its own ingress would reject. `make lint` clean (**first plan this wave where it agreed with `-D clippy::all`**), `semver-checks` **223/223 no update required re-confirmed WITH the new public derives**, `make quality-gate` exit 0 (**249 result lines, 4469 passed, 0 failed**), `make wasm-build` green (83→86, three dead-code entries of the same known D-14 class, baseline MEASURED at HEAD~3 not assumed), `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` untouched. **HTTP-03 stays `[~]`, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched, no checkbox flipped.** **The gap-closure round is CODE-COMPLETE: D-113-L, D-113-M, D-113-N, D-113-O and D-113-P are all closed.** Still open and unowned: **D-113-Q**, **D-113-R** (still blocks HTTP-09 substantively), **D-113-S**, **D-113-T**. **NEXT: 113-28, the decision checkpoint.**

Prior-wave context — **113-31 has landed: the resources half of HTTP-08's opt-in surface is no longer unit-test-only.** Addendum Finding 14(b) measured `grep -rn "resourceSubscriptions|resources/updated" tests/ examples/` at **zero real hits** — two of HTTP-08's four mandated capability opt-ins (`resourcesListChanged`, `resourceSubscriptions`) were exercised only by `#[cfg(test)]` unit tests inside `src/types/subscriptions.rs`, never over a socket, while the tools/prompts half already had live coverage. That asymmetry is invisible in a green suite: the tests that would have failed did not exist. **Four new live-socket tests close it**, and the measurement is now inverted — **21 real hits in `tests/v2_subscriptions.rs`**. (1) `resource_subscriptions_deliver_the_subscribed_uri_and_not_another` pins `SubscriptionFilter::covers`'s `ResourceUpdated` arm — the only arm that scans a client-supplied list rather than reading a flag — as EXACT-STRING selective. (2) `a_resource_subscriptions_stream_is_not_a_resources_list_changed_stream` and (3) `resources_list_changed_is_agreed_and_delivered_when_subscriptions_are_not` are the capability cross-product asserted in BOTH directions: each server advertises exactly one resources half, each client requests both, and each acknowledgement keeps exactly the advertised half. (4) `an_over_bound_resource_subscriptions_list_is_truncated_and_reported` observes the `MAX_AGREED_RESOURCE_SUBSCRIPTIONS` truncation in a real acknowledgement. **Three discipline points make these tests mean something.** The negative case is always fired FIRST — a test that fired only the positive case cannot distinguish "filtered" from "slow" and passes against a `covers` that returns `true` unconditionally, which is exactly what negative control A demonstrates. An OMITTED agreed field is asserted as key **ABSENCE**, never as a falsy value: `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"` keeps `None` off the wire entirely, so accepting `null`/`[]` would pass on a future change that started agreeing to unadvertised capabilities. And the truncation bound is **IMPORTED** from production, never spelled `1024`, so a change to the constant moves the test instead of quietly making it vacuous; the probe URIs are chosen by INDEX (0 vs. the first past the bound) so the test states truncation semantics rather than a coincidence about strings. **FIVE negative controls run, not the plan's four, and each failed exactly ONE of the four tests** — that orthogonality is itself the evidence: a control failing all four would have proven the tests redundant rather than load-bearing. A and B are exact inverses of one another. The fifth (D′) exists because control D — removing `.take(..)` — fails the truncation test at its ACK-length assertion and **short-circuits before the delivery assertions ever run**, leaving "a truncated-away URI does not deliver" as unproven as it was before the test existed; re-running with that assertion weakened caught `mem://r/1024` arriving on a stream whose agreed list never contained it. **Zero production bytes changed** (`git diff -- src/` = 0 after all five restorations; the whole plan diff is one test file, +395/-3), and **no production defect was found**, so the scope fence's record-don't-fix branch was not exercised. **New: D-113-T** — 4 pre-existing tests in this file report intermittent nextest `LEAK` (4 leaks across 12 full-suite runs; **zero** on the four new tests across 16 runs; six runs of the pre-existing 15 alone also clean), caused by bare `handle.abort()` with no await. Measured, attributed and recorded in `deferred-items.md` — **not swept**, because an eleven-test mechanical sweep inside a coverage plan would bury the coverage change it exists to make reviewable. `make lint` clean, `cargo semver-checks` 223/223 no update required, `make quality-gate` exit 0 (**4416 passed, 0 failed**). **HTTP-04 and HTTP-08 stay `[~]`, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched (0-byte diff), no checkbox flipped.** What remains open on HTTP-08 is provenance, not coverage — the advertise-implies-serve rule is conformance-suite policy pinned by 113-32's second gate arm. Still open and unowned: **D-113-T** (new), D-113-Q, D-113-R (still blocks HTTP-09 substantively), D-113-S, D-113-O. **NEXT: 113-27.** *(superseded — see the current entry above; D-113-O is now closed.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-26 has landed: D-113-M is closed — the AAD no longer identifies an equivalence class of requests instead of one request.** `write_canonical` (`src/types/mrtr.rs:1045`) substituted the literal `"__mrtr_depth_capped__"` for everything below `MAX_CANONICAL_DEPTH` (64), so two `tools/call`s agreeing to depth 64 and differing below it produced the SAME `salient_param_digest`, the same AEAD AAD, and mutual acceptance of each other's continuations — replay-prevention **clause 5c silently unenforced** for every request deep enough to reach the cap, and the digest is that clause's SOLE enforcement. **The vulnerability was reproduced against the unfixed tree BEFORE any source change**, so the negative control later had a measurement rather than a prediction to confirm: both digests came out `1bfce28e6995b41583047d92ab099f4b86329e5e2566ce1dc149655b555698f5` byte-for-byte, and the production codec returned `Verdict::Ok(Continuation { state: {"step":1}, exp: 1300, round: 0 })` for a token minted for A and presented on B. **The marker literal is DELETED from the tree** (not worked around — while it exists the branch can be reintroduced); `write_canonical`, `salient_param_digest` and `RequestBinding::from_request` are all fallible over a `pub(crate) struct CanonicalDepthExceeded` whose `Display` names the BOUND only. **`from_request` is the ONLY constructor, which is what forces every caller to decide** — production ingress, production egress, the mint backstop, the `pub` testing seam (`.ok()?`, so both public signatures are byte-unchanged) and the fuzz seam (folded into the EXISTING `VERDICT_UNAVAILABLE` rather than a new discriminant, which would have invalidated archived crash artifacts). **The cap is RETAINED at 64 and only its BEHAVIOUR changed, from aliasing to refusing** — removing it would have traded the confidentiality/integrity hole for the unbounded-recursion DoS T-113-14 capped, so both stay closed; the decision is written into the constant's own rustdoc. **Two typed refusals, both `INVALID_PARAMS` (-32602):** point 1 in `mrtr_ingest` fires BEFORE `codec.verify`, so a request whose identity cannot be computed is not granted a verification attempt at all (and the message differs from `MRTR_REJECT_MESSAGE`, which is itself the structural proof the refusal precedes the tag check); point 2 is `mrtr_egress` step (3b), after the capability precheck and before the mint — `INVALID_PARAMS` and NOT `INTERNAL_ERROR` because the client's params caused it, while the adjacent `INTERNAL_ERROR` channel is for server bugs. `seal_input_required`'s own error keeps the existing `Err(&'static str)` route and is documented unreachable-by-construction, with `the_depth_refusal_precedes_every_mint_precondition` proving the ordering structurally (step 3b refuses with **no codec configured**, which the mint itself would need). Messages are **specific, not generic**: a nesting depth is client-chosen and client-measurable, so it is not an authentication oracle. **The boundary is exact and asserted on both sides:** `write_canonical` accepts a leaf at depth 64 and refuses at 65; through the salient wrapper that means `tools/call` `arguments` may nest **63** levels and the **64th** is refused — and `tests/v2_mrtr_ingress.rs` MEASURES the live cap by walking `mint_request_state` up the depth ladder (first refusing depth: 64) rather than trusting its mirror. Arrays count toward the cap like objects. The **depth asymmetry** is pinned with a compile-time `const _: () = assert!(MAX_INPUT_RESPONSE_DEPTH < MAX_CANONICAL_DEPTH)`: `inputResponses` are depth-bounded at ingress, `arguments` are not, so for `arguments` the canonical cap is the ONLY line of defence. **BLAST RADIUS, stated plainly** — this rejects requests that previously succeeded, but only v2 + codec-configured + MRTR-eligible requests nesting ≥64 that either present a `requestState` or return `input_required`. `a_deep_request_that_never_touches_mrtr_is_unaffected` PROVES an ordinary deep-`arguments` call with no token is `Inert` on v2 and on v1. Measured context: the MCP 2026-07-28 draft schema's whole-document max depth is **12**, its deepest definition **10**, `ElicitResult` **7**, and the deepest of all 109 in-repo `.json` files **18** (a CDK artifact) — the bound is ~5x the deepest structure the protocol defines. **No existing test or example exceeded the cap** (gate green across every example), and the bound was never widened to make anything pass. **NEGATIVE CONTROL — three parts, verbatim in `113-26-SUMMARY.md`, all reverted:** part 1 restored the marker and the aliasing regression failed printing `equal? = true` with the SAME digest measured pre-fix; part 2 showed the deep request MINTING a `requestState` (token visible in the failure body) and the mirror measurement failing; **part 3 is the load-bearing one** — a supplementary live-socket probe in which request B received request A's continuation `{"step":1}` at round 1 and the server answered `"resultType":"complete"` / `"answer":"resumed"`, i.e. D-113-M's harm reproduced over real HTTP. `make quality-gate` exit 0 (**249 test-result lines all ok, 4408 passed, 0 failed**); `semver-checks` **223/223 no-update-required**; `make wasm-build` green (82→83 warnings, the one new being the new type's dead-code entry — the same class its three siblings already had, measured at `HEAD~3`); `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` untouched. **`make lint` again caught what the plan's `-D clippy::all` did not** (`assertions_on_constants`, `format_collect`, `doc_lazy_continuation`) — **fourth consecutive plan this wave**; the first was fixed by promoting the assertion to a compile-time `const _`, which is strictly stronger. Process note for the record: an accidental `git stash push` on the main tree was popped in the very next command with the working tree byte-identical afterwards and the four pre-existing stashes intact; the baseline was then measured the safe backup/`git checkout HEAD~3 --`/restore way. **HTTP-02 and HTTP-03 stay `[~]`, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched, no checkbox flipped.** Still open and unowned: **D-113-O**, **D-113-Q**, **D-113-R** (the last still blocks HTTP-09 substantively). **NEXT: 113-27**, which edits `src/types/mrtr.rs`, `src/server/core.rs` and `src/server/request_state.rs` after this plan and inherits the fallible digest — any new caller it adds must decide explicitly what an unidentifiable request means, and must not reintroduce a fallback value at the cap. *(superseded — see the current entry above.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-25 landed: D-113-P is closed — neither server builder returns `requestState` AEAD key material to the allocator un-scrubbed any more.** `ServerCoreBuilder` (`src/server/builder.rs`) held `Option<[u8;32]>` plus a `Vec<[u8;32]>` of rotated-out keys and dropped both in the clear while every other copy in the module was zeroized for T-113-05; `ServerBuilder` (`src/server/mod.rs`) carried the **identical** field, and D-113-P named only the former — **both are fixed**, because leaving one would have left the documented threat live on the path most users take. The vehicle is `pub(crate) type SecretKey = zeroize::Zeroizing<[u8; KEY_LEN]>`: the destructor rides on the **value**, so it scrubs on drop through moves and needs **no struct-level `Drop`**, which would have made every `self` field move in `build()` an `E0509` and broken the by-value `mut self` chaining idiom the whole builder API rests on (that was D-113-P's own open question; it is decided and recorded in the `SecretKey` rustdoc). **All three enumerated copies are closed and each site says which one it closes:** (1) the FIELDS on both builders; (2) the SETTERS' by-value parameters — `[u8; 32]` is `Copy`, so moving into the wrapper leaves the caller's bytes in the parameter's own slot, hence an explicit `key.zeroize()` / `keys.zeroize()` after the transfer (`Vec::zeroize` also scrubs spare capacity); (3) `resolve_codec_at_build`, which now takes `Option<&SecretKey>` / `&[SecretKey]`, so merely CALLING it no longer manufactures an unscrubbed stack copy. A **fourth, unenumerated copy** was found and closed on the way: `with_previous_keys`'s shadowing `let mut key = key` produced a second `Copy` slot and `zeroize()` scrubbed only the shadow. **PUBLIC SIGNATURES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`mut` on a parameter binding is not part of a signature); only private field types moved, and `semver-checks` is **223/223 no-update-required**. **On the zeroization caveat, stated plainly rather than overclaimed:** zeroize 1.8.2's primitive IS volatile-and-fence-backed (`volatile_write` + `compiler_fence(SeqCst)`), so the overwrite is not dead-code-eliminated — but **no test in safe Rust can observe post-drop memory** (reading a dropped/freed value is UB), so `secret_key_zeroize_replaces_the_key_bytes_with_zeroes` pins the CONTRACT on a value it still owns and its rustdoc says which half is mechanism and which is assertion; `Vec::zeroize` is self-described "best effort" over prior reallocations. The fix bounds what the SDK leaves behind; it does not claim to recover optimiser-made copies, register spills, or swapped pages. **NEGATIVE CONTROLS — both run, verbatim in `113-25-SUMMARY.md`, reverted:** NC part 1 reverted `ServerCoreBuilder`'s two fields to bare `[u8; 32]` and the compile-level guard fired with two `E0308`s naming both fields; **NC part 2 is the load-bearing one** — with the fix reverted AND the guard removed, **all 75 behavioural tests still passed**, including the 113-03 regression `two_servers_with_different_keys_have_different_key_ids`. Behaviour cannot detect a missing scrub, so the **field TYPE is the guard**, one per builder. `make quality-gate` exit 0 (246 suites, **4375 passed, 0 failed**); `make wasm-build` green; `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` **untouched** (728 unique lockfile package names — byte-identical to 113-01's zero-new-crates measurement); `v2_mrtr` 29/29 + `v2_mrtr_ingress` 10/10. Note for the wave record: this plan's clippy break was NOT the usual weak-verify-command case — the plan literally prescribed `&**explicit`, which `clippy::explicit_auto_deref` (in `clippy::all`) rejects; `make lint` was still run as mandated and found nothing further. **HTTP-02 stays `[~]`, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched, no checkbox flipped.** Still open and unowned: **D-113-M** (`write_canonical`'s depth cap collapsing distinct params to one AAD — a hole in replay-prevention clause 5c), **D-113-O**, **D-113-Q**, **D-113-R**. **NEXT: 113-26.** *(superseded — see the current entry above; D-113-M is now closed.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-24 landed: D-113-L is closed — the MRTR round counter is no longer a security bound enforced only by the attacker.** `src/server/core.rs:2230` minted `inputs.round.saturating_add(1)` on every resend and compared it to nothing; the only limit in the tree was `DEFAULT_MRTR_ROUND_LIMIT` (8) in `src/client/mod.rs`, i.e. the D-09 counter was enforced exclusively by the party it exists to constrain. There is now `pub(crate) const MAX_MRTR_ROUNDS: u8 = 16` — **exactly 2x the shipped client default**, so a default-configured pmcp client can never trip it, with the relationship pinned by a `const _: () = assert!(...)` in `tests/v2_mrtr.rs` and exercised by an 8-round legitimate-flow test. **Two enforcement points, and their relationship is MEASURED rather than claimed symmetric.** Point A (`route_mrtr_verdict` → `refuse_past_round_ceiling`) refuses `Verdict::Ok` AND `Verdict::Expired` at or past the ceiling with `INVALID_PARAMS` (-32602, the sibling MRTR reject code, so `v2_status_for_code` → HTTP 400 is byte-unchanged and no new code enters the pre-final `-3202x` range) and a **distinct, informative** message that names the limit — legitimate because a ceiling refusal fires only AFTER the AEAD tag check passed, so it is not an authentication oracle. `Expired` is refused rather than re-elicited because letting one's own tokens expire is entirely within a server's gift, which would have turned T-113-49's round-preservation into the bypass. `UnknownKey` still resets to round 0 and is **ACCEPTED** (T-113-113), documented in the verdict-table rustdoc so it is not re-litigated: it is indistinguishable from starting a fresh operation, which any client may always do. Point B (`seal_input_required`, ahead of every other mint precondition) is an unreachable-by-construction backstop whose `INTERNAL_ERROR` class is correct *because* reaching it means the ingress invariant broke. **The boundary is exact:** presented round 15 still Proceeds and mints 16; presented round 16 is Rejected and the handler never runs — so a raw-frame client resending forever terminates at **exactly 16 handler invocations**, and the deepest round the server ever SEALS (read back out of the AEAD with the fixture key) is 16, nowhere near 255. A proptest over the whole `0u8..=u8::MAX` range asserts `saturating_add` can no longer be observed saturating. **Negative controls — all three run, reverted, verbatim in `113-24-SUMMARY.md`:** NC-1 (A off) terminates but degrades to `-32603` at HTTP 200, and a supplementary probe measured the handler running **17 times instead of 16** — that is the property A owns and B structurally cannot provide. **NC-2 (B off) is STILL GREEN and is recorded as such** — A is sufficient for the client-driven path, so this plan does NOT claim two independently *necessary* points; B earns its place only against a future refactor silently deleting the ingress bound. NC-3 (both off) exhausted its 48-resend hard cap with no refusal — the pre-fix behaviour reproduced. `make quality-gate` exit 0 (246 suites, **4353 passed, 0 failed**); `semver-checks` **223/223 no-update-required** (everything added is `pub(crate)`, a private const, or test code). Two pedantic lints bit that the plan's own `-D clippy::all` command does not catch (`doc_link_code`, `cast_possible_wrap`) — **third consecutive plan in this wave**; `make lint` remains mandatory. **HTTP-02/HTTP-03 stay `[~]`, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched, no checkbox flipped.** Still open and unowned: **D-113-M** (`write_canonical`'s depth cap collapsing distinct params to one AAD — a hole in replay-prevention clause 5c). **NEXT: 113-25.** *(superseded — see the current entry above.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-23 landed: D-113-N is closed and addendum Finding 5 is answered by measurement.** The `subscriptions/listen` route no longer invents an identity: `resolve_listen_principal` implements the SAME three-row table as `resolve_mrtr_principal`, so the two v2 ingress paths on one server finally agree about what an unauthenticated caller is. `(None, has_auth_provider = true)` REFUSES with `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` (-32003) at **HTTP 200** — deliberately unremapped, since -32003 is not in `v2_status_for_code`'s 400 arm and remapping would move every other emitter of that code on the transport — with the original id echoed, `content-type: application/json` (no stream opened), and the refusal placed AFTER both `-32601` gates and BEFORE the params parse, so a v1/capability-less server still answers "no such method" and a refused caller's body is never deserialized. `has_auth_provider` is read exactly ONCE, in `listen_server_view`, through the existing public `Server::get_auth_provider`. **The `(None, false)` row deliberately KEEPS `anon#N`** — MRTR needs a stable principal because it is AEAD AAD, a listen principal is only a concurrency key, and unifying would cap a no-auth server at 4 concurrent streams instead of 64 — with the reason recorded at both sites and pinned by `unauthenticated_listen_still_serves_on_a_server_with_no_auth_provider` (five concurrent anonymous streams, one past the per-principal cap, all served). Negative control ran the expected split (tests 1 and 3 FAIL, test 2 PASSES) and a supplementary control exhibited the harm itself: with the pre-fix row restored, the AUTHENTICATED subscriber gets `application/json` — a refusal body — after 64 anonymous streams, which is D-113-N's stated starvation reproduced on a live socket. `make quality-gate` exit 0 (4337 tests, 0 failed); `semver-checks` 223/223 no-update-required; `pmat` shows **no third** cog-25 violation in the file (D-113-F's two pre-existing ones untouched). **FINDING 5 VERDICT — CONFIRMED, not refuted:** the tag is present and EQUAL to the request id on all three listen frame classes (ack / delivered notification / terminal result) and **completely absent** from an off-stream notification. The off-stream half needed a non-HTTP transport — on `StreamableHttpServer` the listen registry is the ONLY server→client notification sink, since that transport never calls `Server::run` so `notification_tx` stays `None` — so the probe drives a progress notification over the duplex transport and re-encodes it through `pmcp::shared::transport::serialize_message`, the crate's own wire encoder. HTTP-07's CURRENT wording ("delivered on a subscription stream", with the REQUIRED/OPTIONAL note) is exactly what pmcp implements; the phrase Finding 5 flagged was the EARLIER wording, already corrected. **Nothing routed to 113-28 from Finding 5, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` untouched, no checkbox flipped.** Finding 5's ⚠ may be retired from HTTP-07's caveat block at the 2026-07-28 re-verification; **Finding 9's ⚠ may not** (both HTTP-07 obligations are post-RC and open PR #3006 still targets that surface). Verbatim frames and negative-control output are in `113-23-SUMMARY.md`. **NEXT: 113-24.** *(superseded — see the current entry above.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-22 landed: HTTP-09's O(n) half now has a guard that can fail — and it found a new quadratic scan while proving it.** The replaced guard was not falsifiable: `take_utf8_prefix_is_linear_over_a_large_invalid_run` claimed its 256 KiB input "makes a reintroduced quadratic shape hang the suite", and the negative control shows it PASSING in **0.539 s** with the pre-`5f045086` shape restored. That rustdoc is corrected in source, not deleted. The new load-bearing guard is `take_utf8_prefix_stays_within_its_linear_time_budget`: 1 MiB of `0xFF` under a **1 s** ceiling, sized from BOTH sides MEASURED at opt-level 0 — committed 31.8 ms (**31x under**), restored quadratic **9.39 s** (9.4x over) — with the output pinned at the same size so a "fast because it decoded less" regression cannot pass, plus a 4x-step ratio guard (committed 3.46x, quadratic 15.38x, ceiling 8x) that skips LOUDLY below a 50 us resolution floor. Note the plan's estimated ~200x margin was wrong (a release-build intuition; 1 MiB of `0xFF` is a million loop iterations, not one validation) — the measured 31x went into the source rather than the estimate, and the ceiling was never moved to make anything pass. The <=3-byte retained-tail bound is now a proptest over `vec(any::<u8>(), 0..4096)` (residual < 4, residual is a SUFFIX, and one ASCII byte always clears it) instead of three fixtures. Three negative controls run and reverted; `make quality-gate` exit 0 (246/246 ok); `semver-checks` 223/223 no-update-required. **FOUND (HIGH, unowned): D-113-R** — `SseParser::feed`'s `drain_complete_lines` re-scans the WHOLE retained buffer with `find('\n')` per call while a peer chooses the chunking (both incremental feeders call `feed` once per hyper body frame), so one byte per HTTP chunk = one full-buffer scan per byte. RELEASE-build measurement: 5.6 ms / 59 ms / **833 ms** at 16 / 64 / 256 KiB — 148x for 16x input, and 256 KiB is exactly `MAX_LISTEN_LINE_BYTES`. Same class as the CR-02 BLOCKER (1.17 s / 400 KiB), and *worsened* by this phase's larger byte ceilings, since the cost is quadratic in the cap (`connect_sse`'s 16 MiB is ~4096x the work). The plan's own mandated feed budget test does NOT catch it — proven, not assumed: injecting T-113-102's per-chunk full-buffer copy moved it 6.717 ms -> 11.702 ms and it still passed — so that test is documented in-source as a CEILING, not a complexity proof. **HTTP-09 stays `[ ]` for TWO independent reasons:** the publication gate, and substantively — its O(n) clause is not satisfied while D-113-R is open. **Do not close HTTP-09 without fixing D-113-R** (fix shape in `deferred-items.md`). *(superseded — see the current entry above.)*

Prior-wave context — **113-21 landed the enumeration half of HTTP-09.** `tests/v2_bounded_reads_tripwire.rs` (13 tests) scans `src/shared/` (discovered at runtime via `read_dir`, so a NEW file is in scope automatically), `src/client/subscriptions.rs` and `src/server/streamable_http_server.rs` over comment- and literal-STRIPPED text with a byte-to-line map, excluding only `cfg` regions that REQUIRE `test` (per-item brace matching — `streamable_http_server.rs` has two `cfg(test)` fns at 1094/1191 ahead of `mod tests` at 3839, so truncate-at-first-marker would have dropped ~2700 production lines; `any(feature="fuzzing", test)` ships and stays IN scope). The whole-body-read rule is STRUCTURAL — `Limited::new(` must appear in the same STATEMENT — so it fails both on a new site and on a DELETED bound with the site count unchanged (NC-2 proves exactly that). The accumulation rule is documented in-source as a CHANGE DETECTOR, not a proof of boundedness, over a 9-entry / 25-site allowlist whose counts were MEASURED (not copied from the plan: `uri_template.rs` is 11 `push_str` not 9, and `streamable_http_server.rs:4945` is inside `cfg(test)` so that file contributes ZERO) and whose per-entry justifications are enforced ≥40 chars and pairwise distinct. All five mandated negative controls were run against the PREBUILT test binary (a source scanner reads `src/` at runtime, which is the only way NC-5 — an undeclared, never-compiled file — is a valid control), each produced the expected named failure, each reverted; verbatim output is in `113-21-SUMMARY.md`. `make quality-gate` exit 0; full suite 2201/2201 passed. **FOUND (Rule 2): D-113-Q** — the plan's needle families were hyper/axum/std/tokio-shaped and missed `reqwest`; adding `.text()/.bytes()/.json()` surfaced an unbounded `response.text().await` in `OptimizedSseTransport::connect_sse` (`src/shared/sse_optimized.rs:266`) on the FIRST run. That is the fourth-round unnamed site this requirement exists to catch; it is enumerated in `WHOLE_BODY_ALLOWLIST` with a written NOT BOUNDED justification and the list length is pinned at 1, so it cannot go quiet. **HTTP-09 stays `[ ]`** — 113-22 owns the O(n) half, and the publication gate below forbids flipping any checkbox this round. **NEXT: 113-22.**

Prior-wave context — **113-19 (wave 3) landed and the four-plan gap-closure round is CLOSED.** GAP-D: `decode_listen_chunks_for_fuzz` is now behind `#[cfg(any(feature = "fuzzing", test))]`; `#[doc(hidden)]` had hidden it from rustdoc but not from downstream callers or semver. Note for any re-verifier: `cargo public-api` OMITS `doc(hidden)` items, so the plan's seam-absence criterion passed vacuously (it was 0 before the fix too) — the falsifiable proof is a real downstream crate that fails `E0425` under `full` and compiles under `full,fuzzing`. GAP-E: the fuzz target's "latch never clears" tautology is replaced by a per-chunk `buffered_bytes() <= max_buffer_size` assertion, and it is PROVEN falsifiable — with only 113-17's pre-check disabled the campaign stays GREEN (113-17's two enforcement points are independently sufficient), and only with BOTH disabled does it crash (`the parser retained 9 bytes after chunk 0 under a 8-byte bound`). A 20 000-run campaign at `569f3533` is recorded in `113-FUZZ-EVIDENCE.md` § Campaign 2 (seed 3621664529, exit 0, artifacts dir EXISTS and is empty); campaign 1's PASS verdict is preserved verbatim because that campaign was green while GAP-A was open. The cross-cutting phase gate over 113-17 + 113-18 + 113-20 + 113-19 is GREEN: 6 suites, 4 build-matrix rows, `semver-checks` 223/223 no-update-required, zero REMOVED public items, zero new PMAT violations, `make quality-gate` exit 0 (243 ok / 0 FAILED). **NEXT: re-verify the phase** (`/gsd:verify-phase 113`) against `113-VERIFICATION.md`'s GAP-A..E; then, on or after 2026-07-28, re-run the 4-step procedure in `113-SPEC-RECHECK.md` § Recorded Exception, upgrade the Verdict, and only then flip HTTP-01..05 / CLNT-01..02 to `[x]`. A value mismatch is a phase-reopening event. Still unowned: WR-01, WR-02, WR-04, D-113-F..K, UNAS-01.

Prior-wave context — 113-18 closed GAP-B and GAP-C. GAP-B is closed by CONTRACT, not by the originally-planned liveness reclaim: the receiver and the `ListenGuard` share one `stream::unfold` state tuple, so sender liveness cannot observe remote death (the verifier's reproduction dropped the receiver while holding the guard — a state production cannot enter). Instead the duplicate refusal became RETRYABLE (`RATE_LIMITED` -32005 at HTTP 200, `v2_status_for_code` byte-unchanged) and the fresh-id reconnect contract is documented in three places and pinned by a live tripwire whose negative control fails. GAP-C/WR-06 closed: both entry-creating rejection paths route through `prune_after_rejection`, proven by a test that fails when the prune is removed. A re-verifier must reproduce GAP-B through a REAL socket. Earlier in this wave 113-17 landed the parser work — `SseParser`'s bound is now UNCONDITIONAL over `buffer + current_event.data + chunk` (GAP-A closed for real), the two whole-body transport sites go through a `pub(crate)` `feed_complete_body`, and `connect_sse`'s ceiling is a configurable 16 MiB with no public-config-struct change (semver 223/223, no update required). **113-20 has now landed and T-113-84 is DISCHARGED**: `feed_complete_body`'s byte-cap precondition is an established fact naming both enforcing call sites. Every whole-body read on `StreamableHttpTransport` — the POST response, the `start_sse` GET stream, AND the previously-unenumerated v2 error envelope — goes through one `collect_body_within_cap` helper that refuses an over-cap `Content-Length` before reading a byte and bounds the delivered bytes with `http_body_util::Limited` (a STREAMING bound, so an over-cap body is never allocated whole). Zero `response.collect()` remain in that file. `DEFAULT_MAX_COLLECTED_BODY_BYTES` (16 MiB) lives on a PRIVATE field with an additive `with_max_collected_body_bytes()` seam, so semver stays 223/223 no-update-required. Four per-site negative-control runs recorded. D-113-K records the deferred GET-path incremental-parsing rewrite (T-113-94). Wave 3 (113-19, the phase gate) is unblocked. After it, re-verify the phase; then, on or after 2026-07-28, re-run the 4-step procedure in `113-SPEC-RECHECK.md` § Recorded Exception, upgrade the Verdict, and only then flip the seven requirements. HTTP-04 stays `[~]` until that gate clears. A value mismatch is a phase-reopening event.

| 115 | JSON Schema 2020-12 + Caching | jsonschema 0.48 Draft 2020-12 pinned; any-JSON `structuredContent` on v2; additive `ttlMs`/`cacheScope` | SCHM-01..03 (3) | 112 (parallel) |
| 116 | Auth Hardening SEPs | RFC 9207 `iss` (strict v2/lenient v1), DCR `application_type`, issuer-keyed creds + 3 clarifications; no new crates | AUTH-01..03 (3) | 112 (parallel) |

**Execution order:** 112 first and alone → {113, 115, 116} parallelize once the spine lands → 114 sequenced close after 113 (shared stateless-identity/owner-binding pattern) → 117 (needs 113 Client + 114 Tasks) → 118 conformance (validates the union) → 119 docs.

VERS-06 error-code table is structure-first, values-from-final-schema.json only. Open verification item (research): the `-32002`→`-32602` rename direction MUST be re-verified against the final schema before touching the frozen `-32002` task-pending code — cross-cuts Phases 112 and 114.

**Dependency/zero-deps note (research HIGH confidence):** no new runtime crates — only `jsonschema` 0.46→0.48 for Draft 2020-12; Node.js LTS 22.x is CI-only for the conformance suite. Milestone stays additive (2.x minor); `cargo semver-checks`/`cargo public-api` should gate every phase, not just the last (Pitfall 5 — accidental 3.0).

### Decisions

Decisions are logged in PROJECT.md Key Decisions table. Decisions framing this milestone (from design §6 recommendations, approved):

- Boundary razor: contracts + reference implementations in the open SDK; operation + scale stay on pmcp.run.
- Crate name `pmcp-agent` (not `pmcp-agents`); one `pmcp-team-servers` crate with per-server feature flags (not four crates).
- `pmcp-package` adopted into this repo first, published 0.1.0 from here (source: `~/Development/mcp/sdk/pmcp-run/crates/pmcp-package` — import + publish-hygiene, not a rewrite); caret `"0.1"` dep, not `=0.1.0`.
- Legacy inverted sampling kept and documented as the "LLM-server pattern" (no breaking change / no deprecation).
- Sampling-first, not sampling-only: `SamplingSource` (zero-dep) first-class; `OpenAiCompatSource` + `AnthropicSource` feature-gated; three sources maximum, the trait is the extension point.
- The trait seams double as durability seams — the loop stays pure/replay-safe (mirrors the 2.13.0 `poll_decision` non-determinism-inside-the-step design).
- Team-tool contracts as provable-contracts YAML (house convention), namespaced provisional PMCP extensions.
